=== add test/test-tool-progress.mjs
new 114ac491f228175826a07c9b
+/**
+ * Verify the click-to-expand live-output UX for running tools.
+ *
+ *   - On tool-call, a .row-result.running placeholder appears.
+ *   - tool-progress events update the placeholder's summary (byte count).
+ *   - Clicking the placeholder reveals the buffered output as a <pre>.
+ *   - tool-result removes the placeholder and renders the normal result row.
+ *   - tool-progress events are NOT persisted (don't land in session.events
+ *     so a full re-render doesn't replay them).
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-tool-progress.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29010;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', label); }
+}
+function findChrome() {
+  const c = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe',
+             (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'];
+  return c.find(p => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const chrome = findChrome();
+  if (!chrome) { console.error('Chrome not found'); process.exit(2); }
+  const sandbox = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-prog-'));
+  const html = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicSrc = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'agent', 'mosaic-layout.mjs'), 'utf8');
+  const mosaicClean = mosaicSrc.replace(/^import\s+[^;]+;[ \t]*\r?\n?/gm, '')
+    .replace(/^export\s+(async\s+function|function|class|const|let|var)\b/gm, '$1');
+  const htmlWithConfig = html.replace('</head>',
+    '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script>' +
+    '<script>' + mosaicClean + '</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: htmlWithConfig, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm',
+    port: PORT, initialProject: sandbox, modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }],
+  });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1100, height: 800 });
+    page.on('pageerror', (e) => console.log('     [pageerror]', e.message));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+
+    // ── 1. tool-call → "running…" placeholder appears ─────────────────────
+    await page.evaluate(() => {
+      window.renderEvent({
+        type: 'tool-call', verb: 'powershell', name: 'powershell',
+        callId: 'c1', args: { command: 'mvn -q clean install' },
+      });
+    });
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 80));
+    const placeholderExists = await page.$('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"]') !== null;
+    ok(placeholderExists, 'running placeholder appears after tool-call');
+    const placeholderText = await page.$eval('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"] .run-sum', (e) => e.textContent);
+    ok(/Running…/.test(placeholderText), `placeholder shows "Running…" (got: ${placeholderText})`);
+
+    // ── 2. tool-progress updates the placeholder's summary ────────────────
+    await page.evaluate(() => {
+      window.renderEvent({ type: 'tool-progress', callId: 'c1', chunk: '[INFO] Scanning for projects...\n' });
+      window.renderEvent({ type: 'tool-progress', callId: 'c1', chunk: '[INFO] -----< app:portlet >-----\n' });
+    });
+    const afterChunks = await page.$eval('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"] .run-sum', (e) => e.textContent);
+    ok(/\d+\s*(B|KB)/.test(afterChunks), `summary now shows byte count (got: ${afterChunks})`);
+
+    // ── 3. Click → live-output pre is revealed and shows the buffered text ─
+    await page.click('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"] .run-sum');
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 50));
+    const preText = await page.$eval('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"] pre', (e) => e.textContent);
+    ok(/Scanning for projects/.test(preText) && /portlet/.test(preText),
+       `live pre shows both chunks (got first 80 chars: ${preText.slice(0, 80)})`);
+
+    // ── 4. More progress while expanded → pre updates ─────────────────────
+    await page.evaluate(() => {
+      window.renderEvent({ type: 'tool-progress', callId: 'c1', chunk: '[INFO] BUILD SUCCESS\n' });
+    });
+    const afterMore = await page.$eval('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"] pre', (e) => e.textContent);
+    ok(/BUILD SUCCESS/.test(afterMore), `pre updated live with new chunk (got tail: ${afterMore.slice(-60)})`);
+
+    // ── 5. tool-result replaces the placeholder with the real result row ──
+    await page.evaluate(() => {
+      window.renderEvent({
+        type: 'tool-result', verb: 'powershell', name: 'powershell',
+        callId: 'c1', status: 'ok',
+        content: 'exit 0 in 12345ms\n--- stdout ---\n[INFO] Scanning for projects...\n[INFO] BUILD SUCCESS',
+      });
+    });
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 50));
+    const stillRunning = await page.$('.row-result.running[data-call-id="c1"]');
+    ok(stillRunning === null, 'running placeholder removed after tool-result');
+    const finalRows = await page.$$eval('.row-result', (els) => els.map((e) => e.textContent.replace(/\s+/g, ' ').slice(0, 100)));
+    ok(finalRows.some((t) => /exit 0/.test(t) || /12345ms/.test(t) || /\d+ bytes/.test(t)),
+       `final result row present (rows: ${finalRows.join(' | ')})`);
+
+    // ── 6. tool-progress is NOT in session.events ─────────────────────────
+    const eventTypes = await page.evaluate(() => (window.__sessionRef?.events ?? []).map((e) => e.type));
+    ok(!eventTypes.includes('tool-progress'), `tool-progress kept out of session.events (got: ${eventTypes.join(',')})`);
+
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close();
+    server.close();
+    await rm(sandbox, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

=== add test/test-tools-prod-hardening-probe.mjs
new 76400124db64143bcdc0a494
+/**
+ * Regression probes for production hardening of tools + diff (confirmed bugs):
+ *  - read_file strips a leading UTF-8 BOM (Windows editors write it); edit_file
+ *    then matches the model's BOM-free find string.
+ *  - write_file / edit_file reject paths that escape the project root.
+ *  - diff.mjs skips the O(n*m) LCS table for huge files (no multi-GB OOM).
+ *  - StringDecoder reconstructs a multi-byte char split across chunks (the
+ *    powershell stdout decode path) where per-chunk toString('utf8') corrupts.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-tools-prod-hardening-probe.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, writeFile, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { StringDecoder } from 'node:string_decoder';
+// executeForModel, aliased: these assert each tool's ERROR TEXT, which is what the model reads. execute() itself
+// now THROWS for a call that cannot be made (inside a <js> snippet that is what a failure is) — see tools.mjs.
+import { executeForModel as execute } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+import { renderUnifiedDiff, shortDiffSummary, diffTooLarge } from '../src/agent/diff.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ok', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', label); }
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-harden-'));
+  const cwd0 = process.cwd();
+  process.chdir(dir);
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[A] read_file strips leading UTF-8 BOM; edit_file then matches');
+    {
+      const withBom = Buffer.concat([Buffer.from([0xEF, 0xBB, 0xBF]), Buffer.from('const x = 1;\nconst y = 2;\n')]);
+      await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'bom.js'), withBom);
+      const read = await execute('read_file', { path: 'bom.js' });
+      const text = typeof read === 'string' ? read : read.content;
+      ok(!text.startsWith('﻿'), `read result has no leading BOM (codepoint: ${text.charCodeAt(0)})`);
+      ok(text.startsWith('const x = 1;'), 'first line is clean');
+      // edit_file with a BOM-free find string must match the BOM'd file.
+      const edited = await execute('edit_file', { path: 'bom.js', find: 'const x = 1;', replace: 'const x = 42;' });
+      ok(typeof edited !== 'string' || !edited.startsWith('ERROR'), `edit_file matched despite the on-disk BOM (got: ${JSON.stringify(edited).slice(0, 80)})`);
+      const after = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'bom.js'), 'utf8');
+      ok(after.includes('const x = 42;'), 'edit applied');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[B] write_file / edit_file are NOT contained to the project (agent edits e.g. ~/myWlSetup.bat)');
+    {
+      // An absolute write outside the project is done: where the agent may go is the container's decision, not this tool's.
+      const absTarget = path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-abs-escape-probe.txt');
+      const absR = await execute('write_file', { path: absTarget, content: 'x' });
+      const abs = typeof absR === 'string' ? absR : String(absR && absR.content);
+      ok(!abs.startsWith('ERROR') && existsSync(absTarget), `an absolute out-of-project write is done (got: ${JSON.stringify(abs).slice(0, 80)})`);
+      try { await rm(absTarget, { force: true }); } catch {}
+      // A legitimate in-project nested write still works.
+      const inProj = await execute('write_file', { path: 'sub/ok.txt', content: 'fine' });
+      ok(typeof inProj !== 'string' || !inProj.startsWith('ERROR'), 'in-project nested write still allowed');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[C] diff.mjs guards against the O(n*m) blow-up');
+    {
+      const huge = Array(30000).fill('line of text').join('\n');
+      const huge2 = huge + '\nextra';
+      // A ONE-LINE change to a 30k-line file. This probe used to assert it was "too large" — which is exactly the
+      // bug a code review ran into: every edit to a big file came back as counts, including a four-line one. The
+      // guard that matters is the one in the heading — no n*m table, and a fast answer — and a real diff keeps both.
+      ok(diffTooLarge(huge, huge2) === false, 'a one-line change to a 30k-line file is NOT too large to diff');
+      const t0 = Date.now();
+      const summary = shortDiffSummary(huge, huge2);
+      const rendered = renderUnifiedDiff(huge, huge2, { pathLabel: 'big.txt' });
+      const ms = Date.now() - t0;
+      ok(ms < 1000, `huge file, small change handled in ${ms}ms (no n*m table)`);
+      ok(summary === '+1 / -0' && /\+extra/.test(rendered), `and it gets an EXACT diff (summary: ${summary})`);
+      // What stays approximate is a genuinely large CHANGE, not a large file.
+      const ra = Array.from({ length: 6000 }, (_, i) => 'a' + i).join('\n');
+      const rb = Array.from({ length: 6000 }, (_, i) => 'b' + i).join('\n');
+      const t1 = Date.now();
+      const rw = renderUnifiedDiff(ra, rb, { pathLabel: 'rewrite.txt' }) + shortDiffSummary(ra, rb);
+      const ms1 = Date.now() - t1;
+      ok(ms1 < 2000, `a 6000-line total rewrite still returns quickly (${ms1}ms)`);
+      ok(/@@ rewrite/.test(rw) && /approx/i.test(rw), 'and is shown as the file before and after, because the change itself is too large to diff');
+      // Small files still get a real diff.
+      ok(diffTooLarge('a\nb', 'a\nc') === false, 'diffTooLarge false for tiny inputs');
+      ok(shortDiffSummary('a\nb', 'a\nc') === '+1 / -1', 'tiny diff still exact');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[D] StringDecoder reconstructs a multibyte char split across chunks');
+    {
+      const full = 'café €'; // both é (2 bytes) and € (3 bytes) are multibyte
+      const bytes = Buffer.from(full, 'utf8');
+      // Split right in the middle of the € (last 3 bytes): boundary at len-1.
+      const cut = bytes.length - 1;
+      const b1 = bytes.subarray(0, cut), b2 = bytes.subarray(cut);
+      // The OLD per-chunk decode corrupts:
+      const naive = b1.toString('utf8') + b2.toString('utf8');
+      ok(naive !== full, `naive per-chunk decode IS corrupted (got: ${JSON.stringify(naive)})`);
+      // The StringDecoder path (what the powershell tool now uses) is correct:
+      const dec = new StringDecoder('utf8');
+      const fixed = dec.write(b1) + dec.write(b2) + dec.end();
+      ok(fixed === full, `StringDecoder reconstructs the original (got: ${JSON.stringify(fixed)})`);
+    }
+  } finally {
+    process.chdir(cwd0);
+    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });
+

+/**
+ * Unit test for write_file and edit_file in src/agent/tools.mjs.
+ * Run: node test/test-tools-write.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+// executeForModel, aliased: these assert each tool's ERROR TEXT, which is what the model reads. execute() itself
+// now THROWS for a call that cannot be made (inside a <js> snippet that is what a failure is) — see tools.mjs.
+import { executeForModel as execute, extractContent, extractDiff } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+function ok(cond, label) {
+  if (cond) { pass++; console.log('  ✓', label); }
+  else { fail++; console.log('  ✗', label); }
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'code-boss-tools-'));
+  const prev = process.cwd();
+  process.chdir(dir);
+
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[1] write_file creates a new file');
+    {
+      const r = await execute('write_file', { path: 'a.txt', content: 'hello\nworld\n' });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      const d = extractDiff(r);
+      ok(/wrote/.test(c), 'content reports "wrote"');
+      ok(d && /\+\+\+/.test(d), 'diff has +++ marker');
+      const onDisk = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'a.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(onDisk === 'hello\nworld\n', 'file contents on disk match');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[2] write_file overwrites and shows diff');
+    {
+      const r = await execute('write_file', { path: 'a.txt', content: 'hello\nMARS\n' });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      const d = extractDiff(r);
+      ok(/\+1 \/ -1/.test(c), `content reports +1/-1 (got ${c})`);
+      ok(/-world/.test(d), 'diff shows -world');
+      ok(/\+MARS/.test(d), 'diff shows +MARS');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[3] edit_file replaces exactly one occurrence');
+    {
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', { path: 'a.txt', find: 'MARS', replace: 'EARTH' });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      const d = extractDiff(r);
+      ok(/edited/.test(c), `content reports "edited" (got ${c})`);
+      ok(/-MARS/.test(d), 'diff shows -MARS');
+      ok(/\+EARTH/.test(d), 'diff shows +EARTH');
+      const onDisk = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'a.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(onDisk === 'hello\nEARTH\n', 'file edited on disk');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[4] edit_file fails when find string is missing');
+    {
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', { path: 'a.txt', find: 'NOPE', replace: 'YES' });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      ok(typeof r === 'string' && r.startsWith('ERROR:'), `returns ERROR string (got ${typeof r === 'string' ? r : JSON.stringify(r)})`);
+      ok(/not found/.test(c), `mentions "not found" (got ${c})`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[5] edit_file fails when find string is ambiguous (>1 occurrence)');
+    {
+      await execute('write_file', { path: 'b.txt', content: 'X\nX\nY\n' });
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', { path: 'b.txt', find: 'X', replace: 'Z' });
+      ok(typeof r === 'string' && r.startsWith('ERROR:'), 'returns ERROR string');
+      ok(/more than once/.test(extractContent(r)), 'mentions "more than once"');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6a] edit_file: file has CRLF, find given as LF — should still match');
+    {
+      // Simulate the production failure: model copies content from a
+      // read result (LF) and asks us to edit a Windows file (CRLF).
+      const { writeFile } = await import('node:fs/promises');
+      await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'crlf.txt'), '<library-ref>\r\n  <library-name>foo</library-name>\r\n</library-ref>\r\n');
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', {
+        path: 'crlf.txt',
+        find: '<library-ref>\n  <library-name>foo</library-name>\n</library-ref>',
+        replace: '<!-- removed -->',
+      });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      ok(/edited/.test(c), `match after CRLF normalization (got ${c})`);
+      ok(/matched after CRLF fallback/.test(c), `notes the normalization (got ${c})`);
+      const onDisk = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'crlf.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(onDisk.startsWith('<!-- removed -->'), `file rewritten on disk (got ${JSON.stringify(onDisk.slice(0, 40))})`);
+      ok(onDisk.includes('\r\n'), 'preserved CRLF line endings on the surrounding content');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6b] edit_file: file has LF, find given as CRLF — should still match');
+    {
+      const { writeFile } = await import('node:fs/promises');
+      await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'lf.txt'), 'alpha\nbeta\ngamma\n');
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', {
+        path: 'lf.txt',
+        find: 'alpha\r\nbeta\r\ngamma',
+        replace: 'one\ntwo\nthree',
+      });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      ok(/edited/.test(c), `match after LF normalization (got ${c})`);
+      ok(/matched after LF fallback/.test(c), `notes the normalization (got ${c})`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6c] edit_file: model HTML-escaped the angle brackets editing XML (pom.xml bug)');
+    {
+      const { writeFile } = await import('node:fs/promises');
+      // CRLF pom.xml (entities + line-endings combo — the real production case).
+      await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'pom.xml'), '<project>\r\n  <jackson.version>2.20.0</jackson.version>\r\n</project>\r\n');
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', {
+        path: 'pom.xml',
+        find: '&lt;jackson.version&gt;2.20.0&lt;/jackson.version&gt;',
+        replace: '&lt;jackson.version&gt;2.21.1&lt;/jackson.version&gt;',
+      });
+      const c = extractContent(r);

+      ok(onDisk.includes('<jackson.version>2.21.1</jackson.version>'), 'file gets LITERAL angle brackets, not entities');
+      ok(!onDisk.includes('&lt;'), 'no "&lt;" entity text leaked into the file');
+      ok(onDisk.includes('\r\n'), 'CRLF preserved');
+      // A clean miss must still report the file was NOT modified (so the agent knows it failed).
+      const miss = extractContent(await execute('edit_file', { path: 'pom.xml', content: undefined, find: '<absent>x</absent>', replace: 'y' }));
+      ok(/not found/.test(miss) && /NOT modified/.test(miss), `genuine miss is loud about NOT modifying (got ${miss})`);
+      ok(/string-match failure, NOT a permissions\/mode issue/.test(miss), `miss explains it's a match failure, not a mode issue (anti-hallucination hint)`);
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6d] edit_file: model wrapped <find> in CDATA + used literal \\t (the milconnect/pom.xml bug)');
+    {
+      const { writeFile } = await import('node:fs/promises');
+      // The file has a real tab before <parent>; the model emitted "<![CDATA[\t<parent>]]>"
+      // (CDATA wrapper + a literal backslash-t) — must still match via the fallbacks.
+      await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'pom2.xml'), '<project>\n\t<parent>old</parent>\n</project>\n');
+      const r = await execute('edit_file', {
+        path: 'pom2.xml',
+        find: '<![CDATA[\\t<parent>old</parent>]]>',
+        replace: '<![CDATA[\\t<parent>new</parent>]]>',
+      });
+      const c = extractContent(r);
+      ok(/edited/.test(c) && !/ERROR/.test(c), `CDATA + \\t find matched via fallback (got ${c})`);
+      const onDisk = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'pom2.xml'), 'utf8');
+      ok(onDisk.includes('\t<parent>new</parent>'), 'file gets a REAL tab + literal angle brackets (no CDATA, no backslash-t)');
+      ok(!onDisk.includes('CDATA') && !onDisk.includes('\\t'), 'no CDATA wrapper or "\\t" text leaked into the file');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[7] write_file creates parent directories');
+    {
+      const r = await execute('write_file', { path: 'nested/deep/c.txt', content: 'nested ok\n' });
+      ok(!/ERROR/.test(extractContent(r)), 'no error');
+      const onDisk = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'nested', 'deep', 'c.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(onDisk === 'nested ok\n', 'nested file on disk');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[8] write_file to an ABSOLUTE path OUTSIDE the project is done (the container decides what is reachable)');
+    {
+      const outside = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'code-boss-outside-'));
+      try {
+        const target = path.join(outside, 'myWlSetup.bat');
+        const r = await execute('write_file', { path: target, content: 'set MY_WL_SERVER=\n' });
+        const c = extractContent(r);
+        let wrote = ''; try { wrote = await readFile(target, 'utf8'); } catch { }
+        ok(!/ERROR/.test(c) && /MY_WL_SERVER/.test(wrote), `an out-of-project write is done, with no approval to ask for (got ${c.slice(0, 90)})`);
+        const own = extractContent(await execute('write_file', { path: '.code_boss/tasks/x.json', content: '{}' }));
+        ok(/ERROR/.test(own) && /control state/.test(own), 'code_boss\'s own records are still not written over');
+      } finally { await rm(outside, { recursive: true, force: true }); }
+    }
+    console.log('\n[9] write_file/edit_file strip leaked CB-artifact tags (no disk corruption)');
+    {
+      const A = 'antml';
+      // Haiku leaked a stray "foo();</antml>" closer into code; the old colon-only strip missed
+      // the BARE form, so it landed in the file and broke node's parse (SyntaxError next run).
+      const r1 = await execute('write_file', { path: 'leak.js', content: 'const x = 1;\nfoo();</' + A + '>\n' });
+      ok(!/ERROR/.test(extractContent(r1)), 'write with a leaked bare </antml> succeeds');
+      const d1 = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'leak.js'), 'utf8');
+      ok(!d1.includes(A), `bare </antml> stripped from written file (got ${JSON.stringify(d1)})`);
+      ok(d1 === 'const x = 1;\nfoo();\n', 'surrounding code preserved exactly');
+
+      // The pre-existing case the strip was built for: a stray </plan> in content. Still stripped — but now only
+      // when the model ACTUALLY left a <plan> open, which the parser passes in as __cbOpenTags. Stripping it
+      // unconditionally is what deleted <phase>clean</phase> from every Maven pom (see test-cb-tag-strip).
+      await execute('write_file', { path: 'leak2.txt', content: 'real text</plan>\n', __cbOpenTags: ['plan'] });
+      const d2 = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'leak2.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(d2 === 'real text\n', 'stray </plan> stripped when a <plan> was left open above');
+      // The SAME bytes with nothing open are the file's own content, and survive.
+      await execute('write_file', { path: 'leak2b.txt', content: 'real text</plan>\n' });
+      const d2b = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'leak2b.txt'), 'utf8');
+      ok(d2b === 'real text</plan>\n', 'and written VERBATIM when nothing was open — the difference is evidence, not shape');
+
+      // edit_file: the leaked tag rides in the REPLACE string.
+      await execute('write_file', { path: 'edit-leak.js', content: 'a\nMARKER\nb\n' });
+      await execute('edit_file', { path: 'edit-leak.js', find: 'MARKER', replace: 'done();</' + A + '>' });
+      const d3 = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'edit-leak.js'), 'utf8');
+      ok(!d3.includes(A) && d3.includes('done();'), `</antml> stripped from edit replace (got ${JSON.stringify(d3)})`);
+
+      // Must NOT over-strip a legitimate identifier that merely contains the word.
+      await execute('write_file', { path: 'kw.js', content: 'const ' + A + 'Parser = init();\n' });
+      const d4 = await readFile(path.join(dir, 'kw.js'), 'utf8');
+      ok(d4 === 'const ' + A + 'Parser = init();\n', 'bare word "antml" in code is NOT stripped');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[10] execute() coerces a PLUGIN tool\'s declared scalar args from strings (the headless="false" bug)');
+    {
+      // A tool-call attribute arrives as a STRING; the parser only type-coerces BUILT-IN tools (it looks the type up
+      // in the built-in registry), so a plugin\'s boolean flag stayed "false" and `!!"false"` launched headless anyway.
+      // execute() now coerces PRESENT string args to their declared scalar type for plugin (extraTools) dispatch too.
+      let seen = null;
+      const extra = { browser_open: {
+        verb: 'browser_open', name: 'browser_open',
+        schema: { parameters: { type: 'object', properties: { url: { type: 'string' }, headless: { type: 'boolean' }, tries: { type: 'integer' } }, required: [] } },
+        impl: async (a) => { seen = a; return 'ok'; },
+      } };
+      await execute('browser_open', { url: 'x', headless: 'false', tries: '3' }, extra, {});
+      ok(seen.headless === false && !seen.headless, 'a string "false" boolean flag becomes boolean false (not truthy)');
+      ok(seen.tries === 3 && typeof seen.tries === 'number', 'a string "3" integer arg becomes the number 3');
+      await execute('browser_open', { url: 'x', headless: 'true' }, extra, {});
+      ok(seen.headless === true, 'a string "true" boolean flag becomes boolean true');
+      await execute('browser_open', { url: 'x' }, extra, {});
+      ok(!('headless' in seen) || seen.headless === undefined, 'an ABSENT flag stays undefined (impl default applies)');
+      await execute('browser_open', { url: 'x', headless: true }, extra, {});
+      ok(seen.headless === true, 'an already-boolean flag is untouched (idempotent)');
+    }
+  } finally {
+    process.chdir(prev);
+    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e); process.exit(2); });

=== add test/test-trailing-prose-memory.mjs
new 83feb66b6a5a16f3ab44846e
+/**
+ * PROSE AFTER THE LAST TOOL BLOCK GOES NOWHERE — not to the model, not to the chat.
+ *
+ * Reproduced on a fresh conversation, second message: the reply was "lead-in · <checklist> · two questions for
+ * the developer", and the very next turn asked THE SAME TWO QUESTIONS reworded. The questions were shown in the
+ * chat and kept out of the transcript, so the model could not know it had already asked — and each re-ask was
+ * itself trailing prose, so that was hidden from it too.
+ *
+ * Showing it to both parties and showing it to neither are each coherent; showing it to ONE is what broke. It
+ * goes to neither, because nothing useful can be written there: the block's results have not come back, so the
+ * sentence is a guess about output never seen or a question the run will not pause for — and the model gets
+ * another turn, with the results, either way.
+ *
+ * Prose BETWEEN two blocks is different and is kept: it is the required lead-in for the block that follows.
+ * Run: node test/test-trailing-prose-memory.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, writeFile, rm } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+async function run(turns) {
+  const mod = await import('../src/agent/minimal-worker.mjs?v=' + Math.random());
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-tp-'));
+  await writeFile(path.join(dir, 'f.txt'), 'x');
+  let i = 0; const reqs = []; const prose = [];
+  const adapter = {
+    async *completeStream(req) {
+      reqs.push((req.messages || []).map((m) => String(m.content || '')).join('\n'));
+      const t = turns[Math.min(i++, turns.length - 1)];
+      for (let o = 0; o < t.length; o += 60) yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: t.slice(o, o + 60) } }] };
+    },
+    async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: turns[Math.min(i++, turns.length - 1)] } }] }; },
+  };
+  await mod.runMinimalWorker({ adapter, model: 'm', signal: new AbortController().signal, messages: [{ content: 'go' }], projectDir: dir, maxTurns: 4, onChatEvent: (e) => { if (e.type === 'assistant-text') prose.push(String(e.content || '')); }, onPhase: () => {}, onLog: () => {} });
+  await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {});
+  return { reqs, prose };
+}
+
+console.log('\n[1] prose written AFTER a block reaches the next turn');
+{
+  const { reqs, prose } = await run([
+    'Reading the file.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nTwo quick questions before I draft the spec: which flow are we mocking, and vanilla CSS or a framework?',
+    'Thanks — waiting on your answer.',
+  ]);
+  const turn2 = reqs[1] || '';
+  ok(!/Two quick questions before I draft the spec/.test(turn2), 'the tail is not in the transcript');
+  ok(!prose.some((p) => /Two quick questions before I draft the spec/.test(p)), 'and it was never shown in the chat either');
+  ok(/Reading the file\./.test(turn2), 'the lead-in before the block is still there');
+  ok(/<remote-workspace>/.test(turn2), 'as is the block itself, so the results line up with a call it can see');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] a FABRICATED result after the block is still dropped');
+{
+  // That was the reason the tail was being cut: a model that writes its own <remote-workspace-result> would
+  // otherwise be shown its invention as though the workspace had said it. Prose stays, fabrication goes.
+  const { reqs, prose } = await run([
+    'Checking.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\n<remote-workspace-result op="read" status="ok">TOTALLY MADE UP</remote-workspace-result>\nSo that file is fine.',
+    'ok',
+  ]);
+  const turn2 = reqs[1] || '';
+  ok(!/TOTALLY MADE UP/.test(turn2), 'the invented result is NOT in the transcript');
+  // Nor is anything it wrote AFTER inventing that result: parseResponseSegments truncates there, and rightly so
+  // — a sentence written on the strength of a fabricated tool output is a conclusion drawn from nothing. What
+  // this test pins is that the truncation happens at the FABRICATION, not at the last block.
+  ok(!/So that file is fine\./.test(turn2), 'and neither is the conclusion it drew from its own invention');
+  ok(/Checking\./.test(turn2), 'while the prose BEFORE the block is untouched');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] prose between two blocks is kept as well');
+{
+  const { reqs, prose } = await run([
+    'First.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nMiddle note worth keeping.\n<remote-workspace><read path="f.txt"/></remote-workspace>\nClosing thought.',
+    'done',
+  ]);
+  const turn2 = reqs[1] || '';
+  ok(/Middle note worth keeping/.test(turn2), 'the interstitial prose is kept — it is the lead-in for the second block');
+  ok(prose.some((p) => /Middle note worth keeping/.test(p)), 'and it is shown in the chat, while its block runs');
+  ok(!/Closing thought/.test(turn2), 'the tail after the LAST block is not in the transcript');
+  ok(!prose.some((p) => /Closing thought/.test(p)), 'and not in the chat either — same rule, both places');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[4] an open checklist does NOT tell it the task is unfinished');
+{
+  // The prompt says: write the checklist FIRST for ordered work, then do one step and stop. midTask used to
+  // count open items as "unfinished", so from the first tool call onward every turn ended with "continue with
+  // the next step — the task is not finished". The harness was contradicting the prompt on every turn, and in
+  // production that is what turned "I asked two questions" into "I asked them twice".
+  process.env.CB_CHECKLIST = '1';
+  const { reqs, prose } = await run([
+    '<remote-workspace><checklist>write the spec\nread the fields\nbuild the page</checklist></remote-workspace>',
+    'Two questions before I start: which flow, and vanilla CSS or a framework?',
+  ]);
+  // Look at the trailer after the CHECKLIST result specifically — the priming few-shot contains the midtask
+  // wording by design, so scanning the whole request would match the demo instead of the live turn.
+  const req = reqs[1] || '';
+  const at = req.lastIndexOf('op="checklist"');
+  const live = at >= 0 ? req.slice(at, at + 600) : '';
+  ok(at >= 0, 'the checklist result is in the next turn');
+  ok(!/the task is not finished/.test(live),
+    'a list with items left on it is the normal state of a staged walk-through, not unfinished business');
+  ok(/continue, or answer the developer/.test(live),
+    'the ordinary trailer still offers the stop-branch, so a question to the developer can end the run');
+}
+
+console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);

=== add test/test-tuning-settings.mjs
new 0796acf494312a54a5d6ae0c
+/**
+ * THE TWO KNOBS THAT DECIDE WHETHER A LONG TURN SURVIVES.
+ *
+ * Both were env-only, which on a locked-down box means not settable at all:
+ *   - the LLM call IDLE timeout: how long one call may go silent mid-stream before it is abandoned. NOT the run
+ *     timeout, which stays an hour — this fires while the endpoint is still connected but sending nothing.
+ *     The adapters' own default was 120s, and production has shown a 102-second silence inside an otherwise
+ *     healthy answer: an 18-second margin over a thing we have measured. Now 3 minutes, ceiling 10.
+ *   - the COMPACTION ceiling: context size at which the transcript is summarised. 0 keeps the automatic rule
+ *     (75% of the active model's real window) so the default follows the model instead of a fixed number.
+ *
+ * The tests that matter are the ones that prove a saved value actually REACHES the thing it configures: the
+ * adapters read the idle timeout from the environment on every call, and the worker takes the ceiling as an
+ * option. A setting that persists but never arrives is worse than no setting, because it looks applied.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-tuning-settings.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+const HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-tuning-home-'));
+process.env.CB_HOME = HOME;
+delete process.env.CB_LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS;
+delete process.env.CB_COMPACT_TOKENS;
+
+const { startServer } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+const PORT = 29283;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const post = (body) => fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/tuning`, {
+  method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify(body),
+});
+const state = async () => (await (await fetch(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/api/state`)).json());
+
+async function main() {
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-tuning-'));
+  const { server } = await startServer({
+    html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+    defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' } }] }; } },
+    modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir,
+  });
+  try {
+    console.log('\n[1] the defaults are the documented ones, and they are visible');
+    {
+      const s = await state();
+      ok(s.llmIdleTimeoutMs === 180000, 'LLM call idle timeout defaults to 3 min (' + s.llmIdleTimeoutMs + ')');
+      // ONE STREAM: the agent's goal (what its requests are kept within by fading) is auto, which is 200k
+      ok(s.goalTokens === 0 && s.goalAuto === 200000 && s.compactTokens === undefined, 'the agent\'s goal defaults to auto, 200k; no compaction ceiling any more (' + s.goalAuto + ')');
+      // The RUN timeout is a different thing and stays an hour: a WebLogic build chain legitimately takes that.
+      ok(s.timeoutMs === 8 * 3600000, 'the run timeout is untouched at its 8-hour default (' + s.timeoutMs + ')');
+      // The stall cutoff: persisted, broadcast, and effective for the next call through the env the reader consults.
+      ok(s.stallCutMs === 8000, 'the stall cutoff defaults to 8s (' + s.stallCutMs + ')');
+      const sc = await post({ stallCutMs: 12000 });
+      ok(sc.status === 200 && (await sc.json()).stallCutMs === 12000, 'setting it to 12s is acknowledged');
+      ok(process.env.CB_STALL_CUT_MS === '12000', 'and applied through the env the stream reader consults per call');
+      ok((await state()).stallCutMs === 12000, 'and in state');
+      const bad = await post({ stallCutMs: 90000 });
+      ok(bad.status === 400, 'over a minute is refused');
+      ok((await post({ stallCutMs: 0 })).status === 200 && process.env.CB_STALL_CUT_MS === '0', '0 switches the cut off');
+      ok(typeof s.modelContextTokens === 'number', 'the model window is exposed so the UI can show what auto resolves to');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[2] a change reaches the ADAPTERS, which read the environment on every call');
+    {
+      const r = await (await post({ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 600000 })).json();
+      ok(r.ok && r.llmIdleTimeoutMs === 600000, 'the endpoint accepted the 10-minute maximum');
+      // This is the assertion with teeth: openai-http and anthropic-http both read CB_LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS at
+      // call time, so unless the server writes it there the new value never affects a single request.
+      ok(process.env.CB_LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS === '600000', 'and it is in the environment the adapters actually read (' + process.env.CB_LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS + ')');
+      ok((await state()).llmIdleTimeoutMs === 600000, 'and the UI state reflects it');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[3] zero DISABLES the guard rather than meaning "instantly"');
+    {
+      // The adapters treat <=0 as off. A validator that rejected 0, or a server that coerced it to a default,
+      // would turn "switch this off" into "abandon every call at once" — the worst possible misreading.
+      await post({ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 0 });
+      ok(process.env.CB_LLM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS === '0', 'zero is passed through verbatim');
+      ok((await state()).llmIdleTimeoutMs === 0, 'and held as zero, not replaced by the default');
+      await post({ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 600000 });
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[4] the agent\'s goal is stored and surfaced');
+    {
+      const r = await (await post({ goalTokens: 400000 })).json();
+      ok(r.ok && r.goalTokens === 400000, 'the endpoint accepted it');
+      ok((await state()).goalTokens === 400000, 'and the UI state carries it');
+      await post({ goalTokens: 0 });
+      ok((await state()).goalTokens === 0, 'and 0 still selects auto');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[5] out-of-range values are refused, and refused WITHOUT changing anything');
+    {
+      const before = await state();
+      for (const [body, what] of [
+        [{ llmIdleTimeoutMs: -1 }, 'negative idle timeout'],
+        [{ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 900000 }, 'idle timeout over the 10-minute ceiling'],
+        [{ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 99999999 }, 'absurd idle timeout'],
+        [{ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 'soon' }, 'non-numeric idle timeout'],
+        [{ goalTokens: -5 }, 'negative goal'],
+        [{ goalTokens: 99999999999 }, 'absurd goal'],
+      ]) {
+        const res = await post(body);
+        ok(res.status === 400, what + ' is rejected (' + res.status + ')');
+      }
+      const after = await state();
+      ok(after.llmIdleTimeoutMs === before.llmIdleTimeoutMs && after.goalTokens === before.goalTokens,
+        'and nothing moved as a result of the rejections');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[6] the chosen model is remembered');
+    {
+      // The picker is rebuilt on every load and re-selects the server default, so without persistence a different
+      // model lasted exactly as long as the tab — a restart or an update silently put it back, and the next run
+      // used a model the developer thought they had changed.
+      const r = await (await post({ model: 'm' })).json();
+      ok(r.ok && r.model === 'm', 'a known model is accepted and echoed back');
+      const bad = await post({ model: 'gpt-9-ultra' });
+      ok(bad.status === 400, 'an unknown model is refused rather than persisted into every future run (' + bad.status + ')');
+      const prefs0 = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(HOME, '.code_boss', 'prefs.json'), 'utf8').catch(() => '{}'));
+      ok(prefs0.model === 'm', 'and the accepted one is written to prefs (' + JSON.stringify(prefs0.model) + ')');
+    }
+
+    console.log('\n[7] all of it survives a restart — a setting that forgets itself is not a setting');
+    {
+      await post({ llmIdleTimeoutMs: 240000, goalTokens: 333000 });   // both away from their defaults
+      const prefs = JSON.parse(await readFile(path.join(HOME, '.code_boss', 'prefs.json'), 'utf8').catch(() => '{}'));
+      ok(prefs.llmIdleTimeoutMs === 240000 && prefs.goalTokens === 333000,
+        'both are in prefs.json (' + JSON.stringify({ i: prefs.llmIdleTimeoutMs, g: prefs.goalTokens }) + ')');
+    }
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { server.close(); await rm(projectDir, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+
+  // A MODEL WITH A PER-MINUTE TOKEN LIMIT: a goal that fits it (the developer, 2026-11-02: gemini-3.8-flash allows 500K
+  // tokens a minute, and one run's whole sends at the 200K default came to about 800K)
+  console.log('\n[2] gemini-3.8-flash: the agent\'s goal is 100k by default, the bookkeeper\'s and Heads up\'s 150k');
+  {
+    const projectDir2 = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-tuning-'));
+    const { server: s2 } = await startServer({
+      html: '<html></html>', baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' },
+      defaultModel: 'gemini-3.8-flash', port: PORT, adapter: { async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: 'x' } }] }; } },
+      modelsList: [{ id: 'gemini-3.8-flash' }], initialProject: projectDir2,
+    });
+    try {
+      await post({ model: 'gemini-3.8-flash', goalTokens: 0 });   // (the first server's model and goal are in the shared prefs)
+      const s = await state();
+      ok(s.goalAuto === 100000 && s.bkGoalAuto === 150000 && s.huGoalAuto === 150000, `auto: ${s.goalAuto} / ${s.bkGoalAuto} / ${s.huGoalAuto}`);
+    } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+    finally { s2.close(); await rm(projectDir2, { recursive: true, force: true }).catch(() => {}); }
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n' + pass + ' passed, ' + fail + ' failed');
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}

=== add test/test-turn-anatomy.mjs
new d1f4551891f0f94e5662f2bc
+/**
+ * SNAPSHOT #74 / #75 — WHERE DID THE TURN'S TIME GO?
+ *
+ * "The call took 183 seconds" is not a diagnosis. Waiting on the first byte, generating steadily, and stalling
+ * mid-stream are three different problems with three different fixes, and a total hides which one happened. #74
+ * splits every turn into those three; #75 asks whether a long idle pause is what makes the next turn slow, which
+ * is the prefix-cache-expiry hypothesis stated as something measurable.
+ *
+ * The timings are produced by the REAL streaming path — a mock adapter that actually sleeps between chunks —
+ * because ttfbMs and streamMs are new fields on the diag record and the only thing worth testing is whether they
+ * come out of runTurnStreaming meaning what they claim. The formatting cases then feed synthetic records, since
+ * sleeping for an hour to populate the "over 1h" bucket is not a test anyone will run.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-turn-anatomy.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+
+process.env.CB_HOME = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-anatomy-home-'));
+const { runTurnStreaming, streamDiagSnapshot, streamDiagClear } = await import('../src/agent/turn-stream.mjs');
+const { QUERY_CATALOG, runQueries } = await import('../src/server.mjs');
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+const q = (id) => QUERY_CATALOG.find((x) => x.id === id);
+
+/** An adapter that waits `ttfb` before its first chunk, then emits `parts` with `gapAt` inserting a real stall. */
+function pacedAdapter({ ttfb = 0, parts = ['a'], stallBefore = -1, stallMs = 0 }) {
+  return {
+    async *completeStream() {
+      if (ttfb) await sleep(ttfb);
+      for (let i = 0; i < parts.length; i++) {
+        if (i === stallBefore) await sleep(stallMs);
+        else if (i > 0) await sleep(5);
+        yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: parts[i] } }] };
+      }
+      yield { usage: { prompt_tokens: 120000, prompt_tokens_details: { cached_tokens: 90000 } } };
+    },
+  };
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  console.log('\n[1] the new fields mean what they say, measured through the real stream');
+  {
+    streamDiagClear();
+    await runTurnStreaming(pacedAdapter({ ttfb: 300, parts: ['one ', 'two ', 'three'] }),
+      { model: 'm', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'x'.repeat(4000) }] }, {});
+    const r = streamDiagSnapshot().at(-1);
+    ok(r.ttfbMs >= 250 && r.ttfbMs < 1200, 'ttfbMs is the wait before the FIRST chunk (' + r.ttfbMs + 'ms)');
+    ok(r.streamMs >= 5 && r.streamMs < r.ms, 'streamMs covers first->last chunk and is less than the total (' + r.streamMs + ' < ' + r.ms + ')');
+    ok(r.ttfbMs + r.streamMs <= r.ms + 50, 'the two parts do not exceed the whole');
+    ok(r.promptChars === 4000, 'promptChars is what was actually sent (' + r.promptChars + ')');
+    ok(r.promptTokens === 120000 && r.cachedTokens === 90000, 'usage is carried onto the record for the cache columns');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[2] a mid-stream stall lands in gaps, not in streaming time');
+  {
+    streamDiagClear();
+    await runTurnStreaming(pacedAdapter({ ttfb: 20, parts: ['before ', 'after'], stallBefore: 1, stallMs: 1700 }),
+      { model: 'm', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'x' }] }, {});
+    const r = streamDiagSnapshot().at(-1);
+    ok(r.gaps.length === 1, 'the stall was recorded as a gap (' + r.gaps.length + ')');
+    ok(r.gaps[0].gapMs >= 1600, 'with its real duration (' + r.gaps[0].gapMs + 'ms)');
+    ok(r.ttfbMs < 500, 'and did NOT inflate the first-byte figure (' + r.ttfbMs + 'ms) — that is the distinction the tile exists to draw');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[3] a turn that never produced a chunk is all WAIT, not all stream');
+  {
+    streamDiagClear();
+    await runTurnStreaming({ async *completeStream() { await sleep(60); } },
+      { model: 'm', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'x' }] }, {});
+    const r = streamDiagSnapshot().at(-1);
+    ok(r.ttfbMs === null, 'ttfbMs is null, not 0 — "no first byte" and "instant first byte" are opposite findings');
+    const t = (await runQueries([74], {}))[0];
+    // Reading the NOTE, not the body: the verdict line is the whole point of the tile, and until this change
+    // runQueries dropped it on the floor for every query that computed one.
+    ok(/wait 100%/.test(t.note || ''), 'and #74 attributes the time to waiting (' + String(t.note || '').split(String.fromCharCode(10))[0] + ')');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[4] #74 renders a turn the way a person would read it');
+  {
+    streamDiagClear();
+    await runTurnStreaming(pacedAdapter({ ttfb: 250, parts: ['a ', 'b ', 'c'], stallBefore: 2, stallMs: 1700 }),
+      { model: 'm', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'x'.repeat(2000) }] }, {});
+    const t = (await runQueries([74], {}))[0];
+    ok(t.kind === 'table', 'it is a table (' + t.kind + ')');
+    ok(t.body.columns.join(',') === 'time,ctx,cache,before this call,1st byte,stream,gaps,wasted,call,where the time went', 'with the columns asked for');
+    const row = t.body.rows.at(-1);
+    ok(row[2] === '75%', 'the cache share is computed from usage (' + row[2] + ')');
+    ok(row[6] !== '-', 'the stall shows in the gaps column (' + row[6] + ')');
+    ok(/^[ytm\-W=.]+$/.test(row[9]) && row[9].length === 20, 'the bar is a fixed-width shape (' + JSON.stringify(row[9]) + ')');
+    ok(/Model calls dominated by:/.test(t.note) && /ALL OF THE TIME/.test(t.note), 'and the note names where the time actually went');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[5] the bar never overflows its width, at any split');
+  {
+    // Rounding three independent shares of one total can exceed the width; a ragged column is unreadable and
+    // silently misattributes time. Brute-force the splits rather than trusting one example.
+    streamDiagClear();
+    const recs = [];
+    for (let w = 0; w <= 10; w++) for (let g = 0; g + w <= 10; g++) {
+      const total = 10000, wait = w * 1000, gap = g * 1000;
+      recs.push({ at: Date.now(), ms: total, chunks: 3, chars: 10, gaps: gap ? [{ atChars: 1, gapMs: gap }] : [],
+        ttfbMs: wait, streamMs: total - wait, promptChars: 100, promptTokens: 1000, cachedTokens: 500 });
+    }
+    // Driven through the tile rather than a copied formula: the whole point is that what SHIPS cannot produce a
+    // ragged column, and re-implementing the rule here would only test the re-implementation.
+    const bad = [];
+    for (const r of recs) {
+      const row = q(74).compute({ turns: [r] }).body.rows[0];
+      if (row[9].length !== 20 || !/^y*t*m*-*W*=*\.*$/.test(row[9])) bad.push([r.ttfbMs, r.streamMs, r.gaps, row[9]]);
+    }
+    ok(bad.length === 0, 'every split of wait/stream/gap fits the fixed width (' + recs.length + ' combinations)');
+  }
+
+
+  console.log('\n[5b] #74 accounts for ALL the time, not just the model calls');
+  {
+    // A constructed timeline where the true bucket of every second is KNOWN, so the check is that each bucket
+    // gets exactly its share — not merely that numbers appear. Between call A (ends at 3.0s) and call B (starts at
+    // 20.0s) there are 17 seconds:
+    //   3.0– 3.5  code_boss            (nothing running)
+    //   3.5– 8.5  a tool               5.0s
+    //   8.5– 9.0  code_boss
+    //   9.0–12.0  two tools IN PARALLEL (9.0–11.0 and 9.5–12.0): 3.0s of wall clock, NOT 4.5s
+    //  12.0–14.0  a reviewer pass      2.0s — its llm row lands at 14.0 carrying durationMs, both timestamps equal
+    //  14.0–19.0  waiting on you       5.0s — the last activity was 14.0, your message came at 19.0
+    //  19.0–20.0  code_boss            (assembling the next prompt)
+    // so: you 5.0 · tools 8.0 · other model 2.0 · code_boss 2.0, plus the two calls themselves (2.0 + 1.0).
+    const turns = [
+      { at: 1000, ms: 2000, ttfbMs: 500, streamMs: 1500, gaps: [], chunks: 3, chars: 10 },
+      { at: 20000, ms: 1000, ttfbMs: 200, streamMs: 800, gaps: [], chunks: 3, chars: 10 },
+    ];
+    const chatEvents = [
+      // the worker turns' own llm rows: they ARE the table's rows, so they must not be counted a second time
+      { type: 'tool-call', callId: 'L1', verb: 'llm', ts: 3000, args: { command: 'worker turn 1 · m · 2.0s' } },
+      { type: 'tool-result', callId: 'L1', verb: 'llm', ts: 3000, durationMs: 2000 },
+      { type: 'tool-call', callId: 't1', verb: 'js', ts: 3500 },
+      { type: 'tool-result', callId: 't1', verb: 'js', ts: 8500 },
+      { type: 'tool-call', callId: 't2', verb: 'js', ts: 9000 },
+      { type: 'tool-call', callId: 't3', verb: 'read', ts: 9500 },
+      { type: 'tool-result', callId: 't2', verb: 'js', ts: 11000 },
+      { type: 'tool-result', callId: 't3', verb: 'read', ts: 12000 },
+      { type: 'tool-call', callId: 'L2', verb: 'llm', ts: 14000, args: { command: 'reviewer pass · m · 2.0s' } },
+      { type: 'tool-result', callId: 'L2', verb: 'llm', ts: 14000, durationMs: 2000 },
+      { type: 'user-message', ts: 19000, content: 'carry on' },
+    ];
+    const t = q(74).compute({ turns, chatEvents });
+    const row = t.body.rows[1];
+    const before = String(row[3]);
+    ok(/you 5\.0s/.test(before), 'the developer\'s turn is its own bucket, from the run\'s last activity to the message (' + before + ')');
+    ok(/tools 8\.0s/.test(before), 'tools are measured as a UNION — two in parallel for 3s count 3s, not 4.5s (' + before + ')');
+    ok(/llm 2\.0s/.test(before), 'a reviewer pass is recovered from its durationMs, though its two rows share one timestamp');
+    ok(/cb 2\.0s/.test(before), 'and code_boss\'s own time is the exact remainder');
+    ok(!/llm [0-9.]+s.*llm/.test(before) && !/llm 4\.0s/.test(before), 'the worker turn\'s own llm row is not counted again as "other model"');
+    const counts = (ch) => (String(row[9]).match(new RegExp('[' + ch + ']', 'g')) || []).length;
+    ok(row[9].length === 20, 'the bar still has one fixed width (' + JSON.stringify(row[9]) + ')');
+    ok(counts('y') > 0 && counts('t') > 0 && counts('m') > 0 && counts('-') > 0 && counts('W') + counts('=') > 0, 'and it draws the whole slice — before the call as well as the call');
+    ok(counts('t') > counts('y') && counts('y') > counts('m'), 'in proportion (tools 8s > you 5s > other model 2s)');
+    // THE WHOLE WALL CLOCK: 20s from A's start to B's end, every second in exactly one bucket.
+    ok(/ALL OF THE TIME, 20\.0s/.test(t.note), 'the note totals the whole wall clock (' + t.note.split('\n')[0] + ')');
+    const pcts = [...t.note.split('\n')[0].matchAll(/(\d+)%/g)].map((m) => Number(m[1]));
+    ok(pcts.reduce((a, b) => a + b, 0) === 100, 'and its shares add up to exactly 100% — nothing unaccounted (' + pcts.join('+') + ')');
+    ok(/tools 40%/.test(t.note) && /waiting on you 25%/.test(t.note) && /this model \(the rows\) 15%/.test(t.note), 'with each share right: tools 40, you 25, the model calls 15');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[6] #75 buckets by idle time and refuses to over-claim');
+  {
+    const mk = (atOffsetMs, ms, cached, ttfb) => ({ at: Date.now() - 7200000 + atOffsetMs, ms, chunks: 2, chars: 10, gaps: [],
+      ttfbMs: ttfb, streamMs: ms - ttfb, promptChars: 1000, promptTokens: 100000, cachedTokens: cached });
+    // Turn 0 anchors the clock; each later turn's idle gap is (at - previous end).
+    const decaying = [mk(0, 1000, 90000, 500), mk(1500, 1000, 90000, 500), mk(4000000, 1000, 0, 9000)];
+    const tile = (recs) => {
+      const gaps = [];
+      for (let i = 1; i < recs.length; i++) gaps.push(Math.max(0, recs[i].at - (recs[i - 1].at + recs[i - 1].ms)));
+      return gaps;
+    };
+    ok(tile(decaying)[0] < 60000 && tile(decaying)[1] > 3600000, 'the fixture really does span the "under 1m" and "over 1h" buckets');
+    // Drive the real compute through the live ring.
+    streamDiagClear();
+    const ring = streamDiagSnapshot();
+    ok(Array.isArray(ring) && ring.length === 0, 'ring cleared');
+    const empty = (await runQueries([75], {}))[0];
+    ok(/at least two streamed turns/.test(empty.body), 'with no data it says so rather than inventing a verdict');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[7] both entries are real catalog members with descriptions');
+  {
+    for (const id of [74, 75]) {
+      const e = q(id);
+      ok(!!e && typeof e.compute === 'function', '#' + id + ' is in the catalog');
+      ok(e.description.length > 200, '#' + id + ' explains what it measures and why (' + e.description.length + ' chars)');
+    }
+    const t = await runQueries([74, 75], {});
+    ok(t.length === 2 && t.every((x) => !/threw/.test(x.body || '')), 'neither throws when asked together');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[8] turns survive a restart — the reason the log exists at all');
+  {
+    const { setTurnLogSink, turnLogPath, appendTurn, readTurns, mergeTurns } = await import('../src/agent/turn-log.mjs');
+    const proj = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-turnlog-'));

+    ok(/turns\.jsonl$/.test(file), 'the log sits beside the raw log in .code_boss (' + path.basename(file) + ')');
+

+    setTurnLogSink((rec) => appendTurn(file, rec));
+    await runTurnStreaming(pacedAdapter({ ttfb: 120, parts: ['a ', 'b'] }),
+      { model: 'm', messages: [{ role: 'user', content: 'x'.repeat(3000) }] }, {});
+    setTurnLogSink(null);
+

+    ok(onDisk.length === 1, 'the turn was written as it happened (' + onDisk.length + ' line)');
+    ok(onDisk[0].ttfbMs >= 100 && onDisk[0].promptChars === 3000, 'with the timing and size intact after a round trip');
+    ok(!('text' in onDisk[0]) && JSON.stringify(onDisk[0]).length < 1200, 'and NO payload — this file has to stay cheap to scan (' + JSON.stringify(onDisk[0]).length + ' bytes)');
+
+    // The restart: the ring is gone, the file is not. This is exactly the case #75 could never measure before.
+    streamDiagClear();
+    ok(streamDiagSnapshot().length === 0, 'simulated restart — the RAM ring is empty');
+    const merged = mergeTurns(readTurns(file, 120), streamDiagSnapshot(), 120);
+    ok(merged.length === 1, 'the tile still has the turn to show');
+    const t = q(74).compute({ turns: merged });
+    ok(t.kind === 'table' && t.body.rows.length === 1, '#74 renders it from disk with no ring at all');
+
+    // And a turn only in the ring (no project open) is not lost when the two are merged.
+    const ghost = { at: Date.now() + 1000, ms: 500, chunks: 1, chars: 5, gaps: [], ttfbMs: 100, streamMs: 400, promptChars: 10, promptTokens: 0, cachedTokens: 0, fabricatedChars: 0 };
+    ok(mergeTurns(readTurns(file, 120), [ghost], 120).length === 2, 'a turn recorded with no project open still reaches the tile');
+    ok(mergeTurns(readTurns(file, 120), onDisk, 120).length === 1, 'and a turn present in BOTH is not counted twice');
+  }
+
+  console.log('\n[9] a truncated tail line is skipped, not parsed into nonsense timings');
+  {
+    const { turnLogPath, appendTurn, readTurns } = await import('../src/agent/turn-log.mjs');

=== add test/test-turn-delimiters.mjs
new 1fa73b08461d076ebb078907
+/**
+ * GIVE THE MODEL A LEGAL WAY TO END ITS TURN.
+ *
+ * The flat transport's grammar had opening headings only: a turn ended when the NEXT one began. So the model's
+ * only legal way to finish its own turn was to write the developer's heading — and it did, on 54% of calls
+ * measured locally and on 30 of 30 in one live session. The worst case generated 59,060 characters in 223 seconds
+ * of which 599 were real. The parser cuts at the first forged tag and discards the rest, so none of it is ever
+ * seen; what it costs is the generation nobody reads.
+ *
+ * Closing delimiters give it an exit. And because the headings are synthesized at assembly rather than stored,
+ * every turn in the transcript gains them at once — so the model reads the whole conversation obeying the rule
+ * before it reaches the sentence stating it, which teaches far harder than the sentence does.
+ *
+ * This is the half that works on ANY endpoint. Enforcing it with a stop sequence is separate and needs the
+ * endpoint to honour one (Snapshot #73).
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-turn-delimiters.mjs
+ */
+import { assembleConvo } from '../src/agent/engine.mjs';
+import { parseResponseSegments } from '../src/agent/turn-stream.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+
+const CONVO = [
+  { role: 'user', content: 'add a --verbose flag' },
+  { role: 'assistant', content: 'Reading the file first.\n<remote-workspace><read path="a.js"/></remote-workspace>' },
+  { role: 'user', content: '<remote-workspace-result op="read" status="ok">const x = 1;</remote-workspace-result>' },
+];
+const build = () => assembleConvo({ staticPrompt: 'STATIC', liveTail: 'TAIL', messages: CONVO, layout: 'flat' })[0].content;
+
+console.log('\n[1] every turn is opened AND closed');
+{
+  const out = build();
+  const tag = (out.match(/--- developer ([0-9a-z]{3,8}) ---/) || [])[1];
+  ok(!!tag, `the heading nonce is present (${tag})`);
+  const opens = (out.match(new RegExp('^--- (?:you|developer) ' + tag + ' ---$', 'gm')) || []).length;
+  const closes = (out.match(new RegExp('^/--- (?:you|developer) ' + tag + ' ---$', 'gm')) || []).length;
+  ok(closes === CONVO.length, `each of the ${CONVO.length} turns is closed (${closes})`);
+  // One more open than close: the model's own turn is opened for it at the end and left for it to close.
+  ok(opens === CONVO.length + 1, `and opened, plus one left open for the reply (${opens})`);
+  ok(/--- you [0-9a-z]{3,8} ---\s*$/.test(out), 'the message ends on an OPEN "you" heading, not a closed developer turn');
+  // The closing rule once more at the TAIL, just before the open heading. Stated above the transcript too, but
+  // that is hundreds of KB before the model's turn: not one of 71 recorded replies closed, and this line placed
+  // here closed the turn 2 of 2 times in #78.
+  {
+    const tailTag = /--- you ([0-9a-z]{3,8}) ---\s*$/.exec(out)[1];
+    const reminder = 'Reminder: write your reply once, then close your turn with "/--- you ' + tailTag + ' ---" on its own line and stop.';
+    const idx = out.lastIndexOf(reminder);
+    ok(idx > 0, 'the closing reminder is in the message');
+    ok(out.slice(idx + reminder.length).trim() === '--- you ' + tailTag + ' ---', 'and it is the last thing before the open heading');
+    ok(out.indexOf(reminder) === idx, 'stated once at the tail (the head-of-block rule is worded differently)');
+  }
+}
+
+console.log('\n[2] the rule is stated, and the transcript above it already obeys it');
+{
+  const out = build();
+  ok(/closes with/.test(out), 'the header explains the closing form');
+  ok(/NEVER appears inside a "you" turn/.test(out), 'and says a developer heading never appears inside its turn');
+  // The part that does the real work: by the time it reaches that sentence it has read N worked examples.
+  const tag = (out.match(/--- developer ([0-9a-z]{3,8}) ---/) || [])[1];
+  const body = out.slice(out.indexOf('--- developer ' + tag + ' ---'));
+  const bad = new RegExp('--- you ' + tag + ' ---[\\s\\S]*?--- developer ' + tag + ' ---(?![\\s\\S]*?/--- you)', 'm');
+  ok(!/^--- developer [0-9a-z]{3,8} ---$/m.test(body.split('/--- you')[0].split('--- you ' + tag + ' ---')[1] || ''),
+    'no developer heading sits between an open "you" and its close');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[3] a reply that closes itself is cut there');
+{
+  // The whole point: the model writes its answer, closes the turn, and anything it writes past that is the
+  // document-continuation this exists to stop.
+  const reply = 'Here is the answer.\n/--- you hi6u ---\n\n--- developer hi6u ---\n<remote-workspace-result op="read" status="ok">invented</remote-workspace-result>';
+  const segs = parseResponseSegments(reply);
+  const kept = segs.map((s) => s.content || s.body || '').join('');
+  ok(/Here is the answer/.test(kept), 'the real answer is kept');
+  ok(!/invented/.test(kept), 'and the forged continuation after the close is discarded');
+  ok(!/\/--- you/.test(kept), 'the delimiter itself never reaches the transcript');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[4] the forged-result cut still works on its own');
+{
+  // Belt and braces: a model that forges a developer turn WITHOUT closing its own is the old behaviour, and that
+  // cut must not have been weakened by adding a second one.
+  const reply = 'Doing it.\n<remote-workspace><read path="a"/></remote-workspace>\n--- developer hi6u ---\n<remote-workspace-result op="read" status="ok">invented</remote-workspace-result>';
+  const segs = parseResponseSegments(reply);
+  ok(!segs.some((s) => /invented/.test(s.content || s.body || '')), 'still cut at the forged result tag');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[5] an ordinary reply is untouched');
+{
+  const segs = parseResponseSegments('Just an answer, no tags at all.');
+  ok(segs.length === 1 && /Just an answer/.test(segs[0].content), 'nothing is cut from a normal reply');
+  const withTool = parseResponseSegments('Doing it.\n<remote-workspace><read path="a"/></remote-workspace>\nDone.');
+  ok(withTool.filter((s) => s.type === 'tool-block').length === 1, 'and a normal tool turn still parses its block');
+}
+
+console.log('\n[6] CB_TURN_DELIMS=0 restores the old grammar exactly');
+{
+  // This changes the wire format of every request; the escape hatch has to actually work, because the failure
+  // mode on an unfamiliar endpoint is not knowable from here.
+  process.env.CB_TURN_DELIMS = '0';

+// Unit test for turn-stream.mjs — post-stream segmentation + the live streaming pump (both paths).
+import { parseResponseSegments, formatToolResults, runTurnStreaming } from '../src/agent/turn-stream.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+// ── parseResponseSegments ──
+let segs = parseResponseSegments('Reading the file. <remote-workspace><js>return await read("x")</js></remote-workspace> Now editing.');
+ok(segs.length === 3 && segs[0].type === 'prose' && segs[1].type === 'tool-block' && segs[2].type === 'prose', 'prose / tool-block / prose split');
+ok(segs[1].calls.length === 1 && segs[1].calls[0].verb === 'js' && segs[1].calls[0].args.code.includes('read("x")'), 'tool-block carries parsed calls');
+ok(segs[2].content.includes('Now editing'), 'trailing prose after the block preserved');
+
+// hallucinated result tag → everything from it is dropped
+segs = parseResponseSegments('<remote-workspace><js>return await read("a")</js></remote-workspace><remote-workspace-result>FAKE</remote-workspace-result> imagined');
+ok(!segs.some((s) => (s.content || '').includes('FAKE') || (s.content || '').includes('imagined')), 'hallucinated result + continuation dropped');
+
+// unclosed final block → prose (don\'t run partial commands)
+segs = parseResponseSegments('ok <remote-workspace><js>await write("x", "partial');
+ok(segs.every((s) => s.type === 'prose'), 'unclosed block becomes prose');
+
+// ── formatToolResults ──
+const fmt = formatToolResults([{ verb: 'read', status: 'ok', content: 'FILE BODY' }]);
+ok(fmt.includes('<remote-workspace-result op="read" status="ok">') && fmt.includes('FILE BODY'), 'formatToolResults wraps result blocks');
+
+// ── streaming pump ──
+function streamAdapter(fullText, chunkSize = 4) {
+  return { async *completeStream() {
+    for (let i = 0; i < fullText.length; i += chunkSize) yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: fullText.slice(i, i + chunkSize) } }] };
+    yield { usage: { total_tokens: 7 } };
+  } };
+}
+async function runStream(fullText, useTagLexer) {
+  const prose = []; const proseFinal = []; const blocks = []; let usage = null;
+  const full = await runTurnStreaming(streamAdapter(fullText), { signal: { aborted: false } }, {
+    onProseChunk: ({ content }) => prose.push(content),
+    onProseSegmentEnd: ({ content }) => proseFinal.push(content),
+    onBlockReady: ({ body }) => blocks.push(body),
+    onUsage: (u) => { usage = u; },
+    useTagLexer,
+  });
+  return { full, prose: prose.join(''), proseFinal, blocks, usage };
+}
+
+for (const useLexer of [true, false]) {
+  const tag = useLexer ? '[lexer]' : '[regex]';
+  const r = await runStream('Checking the build. <remote-workspace><js>return await read("pom.xml")</js></remote-workspace> Done.', useLexer);
+  ok(r.full.includes('<remote-workspace>') && r.full.includes('Done.'), `${tag} returns the full text`);
+  ok(r.prose.includes('Checking the build'), `${tag} streamed the leading prose`);
+  ok(r.blocks.length === 1 && r.blocks[0].includes('<js>return await read("pom.xml")</js>'), `${tag} block body captured`);
+  ok(r.proseFinal.some((p) => p.includes('Done')), `${tag} trailing prose finalized`);
+  ok(r.usage && r.usage.total_tokens === 7, `${tag} usage captured`);
+
+  // quote-aware: a `>` inside a quoted string in the block must not confuse block boundaries
+  const q = await runStream('<remote-workspace><js>await write("a>b.txt", "hello")</js></remote-workspace> ok', useLexer);
+  ok(q.blocks.length === 1 && q.blocks[0].includes('a>b.txt') && q.blocks[0].includes('hello'), `${tag} quote-aware: `+'`>`'+` in a quoted string does not break the block`);
+}
+
+// ── abort mid-stream throws AbortError but still closes cleanly ──
+let threw = null;
+try {
+  await runTurnStreaming({ async *completeStream() { yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: 'hi ' } }] }; } }, { signal: { aborted: true } }, {});
+} catch (e) { threw = e; }
+ok(threw && threw.name === 'AbortError', 'aborted signal → AbortError');
+
+// Round-6 regression (R6-1): a <remote-workspace> shown as a backtick/fenced EXAMPLE must NOT be split into
+// an executable block (the execution path must match the code-span-aware display); a REAL block still runs.
+{
+  const exampleText = 'To read a file you write: `<remote-workspace><js>return await read("secret.txt")</js></remote-workspace>` — that is the syntax.';
+  ok(parseResponseSegments(exampleText).filter((s) => s.type === 'tool-block').length === 0, 'R6-1: inline-backtick <remote-workspace> is NOT executed');
+  ok(parseResponseSegments('Reading.\n<remote-workspace>\n<js>return await read("a.txt")</js>\n</remote-workspace>').filter((s) => s.type === 'tool-block').length === 1, 'R6-1: a real block is still parsed');
+  const fenced = 'Example:\n```\n<remote-workspace><js>await write("x", "y")</js></remote-workspace>\n```\ndone';
+  ok(parseResponseSegments(fenced).filter((s) => s.type === 'tool-block').length === 0, 'R6-1: fenced-code example is NOT executed');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

new 8d5a4ecab2b08a1b34ae143d
+/**
+ * Markdown + streaming fidelity (UI-overhaul increment 1):
+ *  - <phase> (and other standalone signal tags) NEVER appear in the streamed
+ *    prose — a MutationObserver records if the tag text ever hits the DOM.
+ *  - The streamed prose never shows a raw "###"/"- " line as plain text that
+ *    later reflows: a header line is an <h*> the whole time (no .md-p with '#').
+ *  - Final render: #..###### → <h3..h6>, *italic* → <em>, **bold** → <strong>.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-ui-markdown-stream.mjs   (skips if Chrome not found)
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29142;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+function findChrome() {
+  const c = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'];
+  return c.find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+}
+function chunkedAdapter(responses) {
+  let r = 0;
+  return {
+    async *completeStream() {
+      const chunks = responses[Math.min(r++, responses.length - 1)];
+      for (const c of chunks) { await new Promise((res) => setTimeout(res, 6)); yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: c } }] }; }
+    },
+    async complete() { return { choices: [{ message: { content: responses[Math.min(r++, responses.length - 1)].join('') } }] }; },
+  };
+}
+
+// Header + signal-tag + emphasis, delivered in pieces that SPLIT the markers
+// (so a naive renderer would briefly show "#", "##", "<phase>…").
+const RESPONSE = [
+  'Here is the plan.\n',
+  '<ph', 'ase>desig', 'ning</phase>', '\n',
+  '##', '# Section one', '\n',
+  '##', '##', '## Deep head', '\n',
+  'Body with ', '*ita', 'lic* and ', '**bo', 'ld** done.\n',
+];
+
+async function main() {
+  const chrome = findChrome();
+  if (!chrome) { console.log('SKIP: Chrome not found'); process.exit(0); }
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-mdstream-'));
+  const html = (await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8'))
+    .replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'mock', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'k', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'mock', port: PORT, adapter: chunkedAdapter([RESPONSE]), modelsList: [{ id: 'mock' }], initialProject: projectDir });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  const jsErrors = [];
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    page.on('pageerror', (e) => jsErrors.push(String(e.message)));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => typeof send === 'function' && !!window.__sessionRef, { timeout: 10000 });
+    // Install observers BEFORE the run: record if the signal-tag text EVER lands
+    // in the log, and if a header line is EVER shown as plain paragraph text.
+    await page.evaluate(() => {
+      window.__sawPhase = false; window.__sawRawHeaderInP = false;
+      const log = document.getElementById('log');
+      const scan = () => {
+        // Scope to the PROSE body — the legit phase-marker (a separate element)
+        // shows the mode word "designing"; only a LEAKED tag puts it in the prose.
+        log.querySelectorAll('.row.assistant .body').forEach((b) => {
+          const t = b.textContent || '';
+          if (t.includes('<phase') || t.includes('designing')) window.__sawPhase = true;
+          b.querySelectorAll('.md-p').forEach((p) => { if (/(^|\n)\s*#{1,6}\s/.test(p.textContent || '') || /(^|\n)\s*[-*]\s/.test(p.textContent || '')) window.__sawRawHeaderInP = true; });
+        });
+      };
+      new MutationObserver(scan).observe(log, { childList: true, subtree: true, characterData: true });
+    });
+    await page.evaluate(() => { document.getElementById('input').value = 'go'; send(); });
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => ['idle', 'done', 'error'].includes(window.__sessionRef?.status), { timeout: 20000 });
+    await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 300));
+
+    ok(await page.evaluate(() => window.__sawPhase === false), 'the <phase>designing</phase> signal NEVER appeared in the stream');
+    ok(await page.evaluate(() => window.__sawRawHeaderInP === false), 'a header/bullet line was never shown as raw paragraph text (no reflow)');
+    const html2 = await page.evaluate(() => { const b = document.querySelector('.row.assistant .body'); return b ? b.innerHTML : ''; });
+    ok(/<h5[^>]*>Section one<\/h5>/.test(html2), '### → <h5> heading');
+    ok(/<h6[^>]*>Deep head<\/h6>/.test(html2), '###### → <h6> heading');
+    ok(/<em>italic<\/em>/.test(html2), '*italic* → <em>');
+    ok(/<strong>bold<\/strong>/.test(html2), '**bold** → <strong>');
+    ok(!/designing|&lt;phase/.test(html2), 'final render is also phase-free');
+    ok(jsErrors.length === 0, `no page errors (${jsErrors.length}: ${jsErrors.slice(0, 2).join(' | ')})`);
+  } finally {
+    await browser.close(); server.close();
+    await cleanupDir(projectDir);
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e?.stack || e); process.exit(2); });
