=== add test/test-worker-retract-e2e.mjs
new 7b54c44b1661e631376ca5c2
+/**
+ * End-to-end: a fumbled reply the worker AUTO-CORRECTS must NOT clutter the conversation. Here the model returns
+ * the genai.mil/Gemini transient-error phrase; the worker retracts it + silently re-runs, and only the REAL answer
+ * remains in the chat. Exercises the retract-assistant path over the REAL server + UI. Run: node test/test-worker-retract-e2e.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, readFile, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { cleanupDir } from './_helpers.mjs';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
+import puppeteer from 'puppeteer-core';
+import { startServer } from '../src/server.mjs';
+process.env.CB_SKILLS_MAINTAINER ??= '0';   // (the skills maintainer reads each run since 2026-10-29; its calls are not this test's)
+
+const __dirname = path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url));
+const ROOT = path.resolve(__dirname, '..');
+const PORT = 29141;
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const chrome = ['C:\\Program Files\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe', (process.env.LOCALAPPDATA || '') + '\\Google\\Chrome\\Application\\chrome.exe'].find((p) => { try { return p && existsSync(p); } catch { return false; } });
+const sleep = (ms) => new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, ms));
+
+// Lenient adapter: per-call scripted chunks; repeats the last script if over-called (never crashes the run).
+function chunkedAdapter(responses) {
+  let r = 0;
+  const next = () => responses[Math.min(r++, responses.length - 1)];
+  return {
+    async *completeStream() { for (const c of next()) { await sleep(4); yield { choices: [{ delta: { content: c } }] }; } },
+    // (the bookkeeper - on by default - is answered apart, so it never takes the agent's next scripted reply)
+    async complete(req) { const all = ((req && req.messages) || []).map((m) => String(m.content || '')).join(' '); if (/bookkeeper of a working session/.test(all)) return { choices: [{ message: { content: '<stream-ops><developer-not-needed/></stream-ops>' } }] }; return { choices: [{ message: { content: next().join('') } }] }; },
+  };
+}
+
+async function main() {
+  if (!chrome) { console.error('Chrome not found'); process.exit(2); }
+  const projectDir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-retract-'));
+  const ERR = "I couldn't find the answer this time due to an internal error in code generation, you can try again later.";
+  const responses = [
+    [ERR],                                                     // turn 1 → transient backend error (fumble)
+    ['Here is the REAL answer: the build succeeded in 5.3s.'], // re-run → the real reply
+    ['Here is the SECOND answer: it issues an HTTP GET.'],     // a LATER message — its turn numbering restarts at 1
+    [''],                                                      // any trailing pass → benign
+  ];
+  const html = await readFile(path.join(ROOT, 'src', 'ui.html'), 'utf8');
+  const htmlWithConfig = html.replace('</head>', '<script>window.SERVER_CONFIG=' + JSON.stringify({ defaultModel: 'm', baseURL: '(mock)' }) + ';</script></head>');
+  const { server } = await startServer({ html: htmlWithConfig, baseURL: '(mock)', apiKey: 'test-key', gitlabCreds: { user: 't', token: 't' }, defaultModel: 'm', port: PORT, adapter: chunkedAdapter(responses), modelsList: [{ id: 'm' }], initialProject: projectDir });
+  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ executablePath: chrome, headless: 'new', args: ['--no-sandbox'] });
+  try {
+    const page = await browser.newPage();
+    await page.setViewport({ width: 1100, height: 800 });
+    const errs = []; page.on('pageerror', (e) => errs.push(e.message));
+    await page.goto(`http://127.0.0.1:${PORT}/`);
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await sleep(300);
+
+    console.log('\n[A] a transient-error fumble is retracted; only the real answer survives');
+    await page.type('#input', 'build it');
+    await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__sessionRef?.status === 'done' || window.__sessionRef?.status === 'idle', { timeout: 20000 });
+    await sleep(400);
+
+    const bubbles = await page.$$eval('.row.assistant', (els) => els.map((el) => el.querySelector('.body')?.textContent.trim() || '').filter(Boolean));
+    ok(bubbles.some((b) => /REAL answer/.test(b)), `the real answer is shown (bubbles: ${JSON.stringify(bubbles.map((s) => s.slice(0, 30)))})`);
+    ok(!bubbles.some((b) => /internal error in code generation/i.test(b)), 'the transient-error fumble was RETRACTED (not shown to the developer)');
+
+    console.log('\n[B] on reload the retracted fumble stays hidden');
+    await page.reload();
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await sleep(500);
+    const afterReload = await page.$$eval('.row.assistant', (els) => els.map((el) => el.querySelector('.body')?.textContent.trim() || '').filter(Boolean));
+    ok(afterReload.some((b) => /REAL answer/.test(b)), 'the real answer persists after reload');
+    ok(!afterReload.some((b) => /internal error in code generation/i.test(b)), 'the fumble stays hidden after reload (isRetractedTurn holds on replay)');
+
+    // Reported from production 2026-08-10: a later message's answer never appeared — persisted correctly in
+    // conversation.jsonl, absent from the chat, and still absent after a reload. taskId is the constant 'conv'
+    // and turn numbers restart at 1 for EVERY user message, so one retract at turn 1 hid the turn-1 answer of
+    // every message that followed it, for the life of the conversation.
+    console.log('\n[C] a LATER message whose turn number collides with an earlier retract still renders');
+    await page.type('#input', 'and how does it call the service?');
+    await page.keyboard.press('Enter');
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__sessionRef?.status === 'running', { timeout: 10000 }).catch(() => {});
+    await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__sessionRef?.status === 'done' || window.__sessionRef?.status === 'idle', { timeout: 20000 });
+    await sleep(500);
+    const after2 = await page.$$eval('.row.assistant', (els) => els.map((el) => el.querySelector('.body')?.textContent.trim() || '').filter(Boolean));
+    ok(after2.some((b) => /SECOND answer/.test(b)), `the second message's answer is shown (bubbles: ${JSON.stringify(after2.map((s) => s.slice(0, 26)))})`);
+    ok(after2.some((b) => /REAL answer/.test(b)), 'and the first answer is still there');
+    ok(!after2.some((b) => /internal error in code generation/i.test(b)), 'while the original fumble is still hidden — the retract still does its job');
+
+    await page.reload();
+    await page.waitForSelector('#chat:not(.hidden)', { timeout: 15000 });
+    await sleep(600);
+    const after2Reload = await page.$$eval('.row.assistant', (els) => els.map((el) => el.querySelector('.body')?.textContent.trim() || '').filter(Boolean));
+    ok(after2Reload.some((b) => /SECOND answer/.test(b)), 'it survives a reload too (the replay applies the same filter)');
+    ok(!after2Reload.some((b) => /internal error in code generation/i.test(b)), 'and the fumble is still hidden on replay');
+
+    ok(errs.length === 0, `no page errors (${JSON.stringify(errs.slice(0, 2))})`);
+  } catch (e) { fail++; console.log('  FAIL harness:', e?.message || e); }
+  finally { await browser.close(); try { server.close(); } catch {} await cleanupDir(projectDir); }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main();

=== add test/test-write-guard.mjs
new 957edd73a8542fb599ddea6f
+/**
+ * The guard between a model-authored body and a real file. Both failures it catches are SILENT — the write
+ * succeeds, the reply looks right, and the damage only shows up when somebody opens the file — so the tests here
+ * are about the two ways the guard could be wrong: missing a corrupted body, and "fixing" a correct one.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-write-guard.mjs
+ */
+import { guardWholeFileWrite, describeEscaping, assessLineLoss, unescapeBody, looksEscaped, noteServedFile, checkStaleBase, _resetServedFiles } from '../src/agent/write-guard.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, label) => { if (c) { pass++; process.stdout.write(`  ok ${label}\n`); } else { fail++; process.stdout.write(`  FAIL ${label}\n`); } };
+
+const ORIGINAL = [
+  'export const PORT = 8443;',
+  'export const HOST = "0.0.0.0";',
+  '// matches a<b|c>d style ids',
+  'export const ID_RE = /a<b|c>d/g;',
+  'export const TIMEOUT_MS = 30_000;',
+].join('\n') + '\n';
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[1] the corruption we actually watched happen\n');
+{
+  // Lifted from a live run on 08-13: the edit landed and the regex beside it was destroyed.
+  const mangled = ORIGINAL.replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;').replace('30_000', '60_000');
+  const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: ORIGINAL, body: mangled, path: 'src/config.ts' });
+  ok(r.ok, 'the write is allowed — the change was legitimate, only the punctuation was mangled');
+  // IT IS NO LONGER REPAIRED, and that is the design. Un-escaping meant deciding whether the transport did it or
+  // the model meant it, which nothing in the two texts can tell — so the body lands as sent and the model is TOLD.
+  ok(r.body === mangled, 'the body reaches disk exactly as sent — we no longer guess at intent on its behalf');
+  ok(/would match the file exactly without them/.test(r.notes.join(' ')), 'and the note names what happened, in the tool result, on its next turn');
+  ok(r.notes.join('\n').includes('without the escapes: export const ID_RE = /a<b|c>d/g;'), 'showing the line as it landed AND what removing the escapes would give');
+  ok(/nothing needs doing/.test(r.notes.join(' ')), 'and saying plainly that a deliberate escape needs no action — a note that pushes makes a model undo a change it meant');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[2] a body whose backslashes belong to the code is left alone\n');
+{
+  // Every escape here is load-bearing. A blanket un-escape would break all of it, which is why the repair is
+  // scored against the original rather than applied on sight.
+  const legit = ORIGINAL.replace('export const ID_RE = /a<b|c>d/g;', 'export const ID_RE = /\\d+\\|\\w+/g;');
+  const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: ORIGINAL, body: legit, path: 'src/config.ts' });
+  ok(r.ok && r.body === legit, 'committed byte-for-byte');
+  ok(!/would match the file exactly without them/.test(r.notes.join(' ')), 'and NOT flagged — un-escaping these would match nothing in the file, so there is nothing to report');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[3] line loss is REPORTED, never refused — there is no threshold any more\n');
+{
+  // THIS SECTION USED TO ASSERT A REFUSAL, and the refusal is gone. It was a percentage: below N% of the file's
+  // lines surviving, block the write. That cannot work, because a legitimate refactor keeps almost nothing
+  // either — a live sonnet run rewrote a 33-line server.js correctly and kept 9 lines, one above the threshold,
+  // with the finished file already below it. Every value is wrong in one direction or the other.
+  //
+  // Truncation is now caught where it is KNOWN rather than guessed: the parser sees a <write> with no closing
+  // </write> and the write is refused for that, upstream in tools.mjs. What remains here is a report.
+  const BIG = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => `export function helper${i}(a, b) { return a * ${i + 2} + b; }`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const cut = BIG.split('\n').slice(0, 3).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: BIG, body: cut, path: 'src/helpers.ts' });
+  ok(r.ok, 'a 40-line file coming back as 3 lines is ALLOWED here — nothing at this layer can tell it from a rewrite');
+  ok(/37 of 40 existing line\(s\) are not in the new body/.test(r.notes.join(' ')), 'but the loss is REPORTED exactly, and the model sees it on its next turn');
+
+  // A small file behaves the same way, which is the point: one rule, no size-dependent special cases.
+  const tiny = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: 'export const A = 1;\nexport const B = 2;\n', body: 'export const A = 9;\n', path: 'src/tiny.ts' });
+  ok(tiny.ok && /2 of 2 existing line\(s\)/.test(tiny.notes.join(' ')), 'and a two-line file reports the same way — no floor, no minimum, no exception');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[4] an ordinary edit passes, and reports what it changed\n');
+{
+  const edited = ORIGINAL.replace('30_000', '60_000') + 'export const RETRIES = 3;\n';
+  const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: ORIGINAL, body: edited, path: 'src/config.ts' });
+  ok(r.ok && r.body === edited, 'a normal one-line change goes through untouched');
+  ok(/1 of 5 existing line\(s\) are not in the new body/.test(r.notes.join(' ')), 'and the line it replaced is reported, so a wrong edit is visible next turn');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[5] the pieces behave on their own\n');
+{
+  ok(looksEscaped('a &lt; b') && looksEscaped('a \\| b') && !looksEscaped('a < b'), 'looksEscaped is a cheap pre-filter, not the decision');
+  ok(unescapeBody('&amp;lt;') === '&lt;', 'entities decode in ONE pass — &amp;lt; must not become <');
+  ok(assessLineLoss(ORIGINAL, ORIGINAL).ratio === 1, 'an identical body loses nothing');
+  ok(assessLineLoss('', 'anything').refuse === false, 'a new file cannot lose lines, so it is never refused');
+  const none = describeEscaping('', '&lt;p&gt;');
+  ok(!none.note && !none.lines.length, 'with no original to compare against there is nothing to say, so nothing is said');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[6] a rewrite built on a stale copy is refused\n');
+{
+  // The failure NOTHING else here can see. The body keeps all the original's lines and the punctuation is fine,
+  // so every other check passes — and it quietly undoes whatever changed while the model was looking elsewhere.
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  const P = '/proj/src/config.ts';
+  const V1 = 'export const A = 1;\nexport const B = 2;\n';
+  noteServedFile(P, V1);                                    // the model read it
+  const V2 = V1 + 'export const FROM_SOMEONE_ELSE = true;\n';  // and then it moved underneath
+
+  const stale = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: V2, body: V1.replace('A = 1', 'A = 9'), path: 'src/config.ts', absPath: P });
+  ok(!stale.ok, 'a body built on the copy it read is REFUSED once the file has moved');
+  ok(/CHANGED since you last read it/.test(stale.error || ''), 'and the error says why, not just that it failed');
+  ok(/Read the WHOLE file again/.test(stale.error || ''), 'with the recovery, since the model has to act on it — and it has to say WHOLE, because a partial read satisfies "read it again" while recording nothing');
+
+  // Re-reading clears it — otherwise the model could never recover and would loop.
+  noteServedFile(P, V2);
+  const after = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: V2, body: V2.replace('A = 1', 'A = 9'), path: 'src/config.ts', absPath: P });
+  ok(after.ok, 'and re-reading clears it, or the model could never get out of the refusal');
+
+  // No record means no opinion — after a restart the map is empty and a write must not be blocked for that.
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  const unknown = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: V2, body: V2.replace('A = 1', 'A = 9'), path: 'src/config.ts', absPath: P });
+  ok(unknown.ok, 'a file we have no record of is allowed — we refuse what we KNOW is wrong, never what we cannot tell');
+
+  ok(checkStaleBase(P, 'anything').stale === false, 'checkStaleBase has no opinion without a record');
+  noteServedFile(P, 'x');
+  ok(checkStaleBase(P, 'x').stale === false && checkStaleBase(P, 'y').stale === true, 'and a plain digest comparison decides it');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7] the shapes of real file this guard mis-reads, and the edges that must not move\n');
+{
+  // Everything above proves the guard on the file it was designed against: a few dozen lines, unique, LF, one
+  // instrument (how many of the original's lines are still there) doing all the deciding. This section is the
+  // rest of a real checkout — BOM-prefixed scripts, minified bundles, repetitive Java, regexes — plus the
+  // constants and orderings that nothing currently pins.
+  const path = await import('node:path');   // dynamic, so the import line at the top of the file stays as it is
+
+  // A BOM-prefixed file must stay writable after being read. read_file strips the BOM before recording what it
+  // served (tools.mjs safeReadText), write_file compares against the raw readFile(utf8) text that still has it,
+  // so the digests can NEVER match: every whole-file write is refused, the refusal says "read it again", the
+  // re-read strips the BOM again, and the model loops on an instruction that cannot succeed. On a Windows repo
+  // that is launch.vbs, every .ps1, every .csproj — anything Notepad or Visual Studio has touched.
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  const PS1 = '/proj/launch.ps1';
+  const ON_DISK = '﻿$Port = 8443\r\n$Host = "0.0.0.0"\r\nWrite-Host "listening on $Port"\r\n';
+  noteServedFile(PS1, ON_DISK.replace(/^﻿/, ''));   // what read_file actually records
+  ok(checkStaleBase(PS1, ON_DISK).stale === false,
+    'a file whose only oddity is a leading BOM is still writable after we read it — the copy we served and the bytes on disk are the same file');
+
+  // Two spellings of one absolute path must be one key. read_file keys absolutes on the raw string it was given;
+  // write_file resolves first. So the check is silently OFF for the forward-slash and dot-segment absolutes a
+  // model emits constantly — not a wrong refusal, NO refusal, with nothing in the output to say it never ran.
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  const RAW = '/proj/src/../src/a.js';
+  noteServedFile(RAW, 'export const A = 1;\n');
+  ok(checkStaleBase(path.resolve(RAW), 'export const A = 1;\nexport const FROM_COLLEAGUE = 1;\n').stale === true,
+    'a path written one way and read another is still the same file — the record normalises its own keys rather than trusting the spelling');
+
+  // A backslash that belongs to the code has to survive damage to the REST of the body. The repair is all-or-
+  // nothing over the whole body, so once un-escaping improves the overall score a correct `\|` goes with the
+  // corruption — the module header's own example of what it must never do. Silent and semantic: a literal pipe
+  // becomes an alternation, so the regex still compiles, still runs, and matches the wrong things.
+  const SPLIT = [
+    'export const SPLIT_RE = /\\d+\\|\\w+/g;',
+    'export function pick(a, b) { return a < b ? a : b; }',
+    'export function gt(a, b) { return a > b; }',
+    'export const OPEN_TAG = "<section>";',
+    'export const CLOSE_TAG = "</section>";',
+    'export const SAMPLE = "a<b>c";',
+  ].join('\n') + '\n';
+  const splitMangled = SPLIT.replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;');
+  const rSplit = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: SPLIT, body: splitMangled, path: 'src/split.ts' });
+  // Repairing is gone; the body lands as sent and the model is told which lines look mangled and what they were.
+  ok(rSplit.ok && rSplit.body === splitMangled, 'a body the transport mangled is written AS SENT — the repair that used to happen here could not tell it from a deliberate edit');
+  ok(/would match the file exactly without them/.test(rSplit.notes.join(' ')), 'and every affected line is named, with its before and its un-escaped form, for the model to judge');
+  ok(rSplit.body.includes('/\\d+\\|\\w+/g'),
+    'and the one escape that was CORRECT survives the repair — a regex alternation is not damage just because the lines around it were');
+  // Same shape, and the one this repo would hit first: `/\.mjs$/` becomes `/.mjs$/`, which matches any character.
+  const EXT = SPLIT.replace('/\\d+\\|\\w+/g', '/\\.mjs$/');
+  const rExt = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: EXT, body: EXT.replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;'), path: 'src/ext.ts' });
+  ok(rExt.body.includes('/\\.mjs$/'), 'an escaped dot in an extension test survives too — `.` matching anything is the same bug wearing a different character');
+
+  // Every escape the repair knows how to undo has to be one the pre-filter recognises. unescapeBody strips
+  // `\[ \] \( \) \# \+ \- \. \!` as well, but looksEscaped never fires on them alone, so a body escaped with
+  // markdown punctuation ONLY is never even offered to the repair that would have fixed it perfectly.
+  const mdEscape = (t) => t.replace(/([()\.])/g, '\\$1');
+  const FMT6 = Array.from({ length: 6 }, (_, i) => `export const P${i} = format(config.name, ${i});`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  ok(looksEscaped(mdEscape(FMT6)) === true,
+    'a body escaped only with markdown punctuation is recognised as escaped — the pre-filter covers everything the repair can undo, or it turns away bodies it could have saved');
+  const rFmt = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FMT6, body: mdEscape(FMT6), path: 'src/fmt.ts' });
+  ok(rFmt.ok && rFmt.body === mdEscape(FMT6) && /would match the file exactly without them/.test(rFmt.notes.join(' ')),
+    'and a small file escaped that way is written AS SENT and REPORTED — the pre-filter must still see it, or the model is never told');
+  // Above the truncation floor the same corruption IS caught, but blamed on the wrong thing — the model is told
+  // to send the file back complete, sends it back complete, and the transport escapes it again.
+  const FMT12 = Array.from({ length: 12 }, (_, i) => `export const P${i} = format(config.name, ${i});`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const rFmt12 = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FMT12, body: mdEscape(FMT12), path: 'src/fmt.ts' });
+  ok(rFmt12.ok && rFmt12.body === mdEscape(FMT12) && /would match the file exactly without them/.test(rFmt12.notes.join(' ')),
+    'a larger one is reported the same way — size changes nothing now that no threshold is left to cross');
+  // The pre-filter used to be the only thing standing between these bodies and the repair. There is no repair
+  // now, so what matters is that the pre-filter still SEES them — a body it turns away is one the model is never
+  // told about, and silence is the failure this whole module exists to prevent.
+  const seen = describeEscaping(FMT12, mdEscape(FMT12));
+  ok(seen.lines.length === 12 && /without the escapes: export const P0 = format\(config.name, 0\);/.test(seen.note),
+    'every markdown-escaped line is recognised and reported with its plain form — the pre-filter reaching them is the whole job now')
+
+  // A one-line file — a minified bundle, single-line JSON, a compiled asset — has no resolution below one line,
+  // so total is 1, lost can never reach 10, and NOTHING is ever refused. An 11KB bundle can come back at 10% or
+  // empty and the guard reports success. This is the catastrophic truncation the check exists for, in the file
+  // shape where a human is least likely to notice by eye.
+  let BUNDLE = '!function(e,t){"object"==typeof exports?module.exports=t():e.lib=t()}(this,function(){';
+  while (BUNDLE.length < 11300) BUNDLE += `var a${BUNDLE.length}=function(x){return x*2};`;
+  BUNDLE += '});';
+  // A one-line file has no line resolution, which is why this used to need its own character-ratio rule. With
+  // the threshold gone the special case goes too: a shortened bundle is allowed here and a genuinely cut-off one
+  // is caught upstream by its unterminated tag, which works the same for one line as for a thousand.
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: BUNDLE, body: BUNDLE.slice(0, Math.floor(BUNDLE.length * 0.1)), path: 'dist/bundle.min.js' }).ok === true,
+    'a shortened one-line bundle is allowed — a rebuild legitimately shrinks, and nothing here can tell that from a cut-off reply');
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: BUNDLE, body: '', path: 'dist/bundle.min.js' }).ok === false,
+    'and an empty body against it is refused for the same reason, rather than deleting the file with a note');
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: BUNDLE, body: 'console.log("regenerated");\n', path: 'dist/bundle.min.js' }).ok === true,
+    'while a one-line file genuinely replaced is still allowed — the signal is that the new body is a prefix of the old, not that the file is short');
+
+  // Survival has to count how much of the file is still THERE. Scoring is substring containment per line, so one
+  // copy of a repeated line in the new body credits every occurrence of it in the original. Repeated lines are
+  // the norm in the languages this tool is aimed at, so the guard is weakest exactly where files are most
+  // repetitive — a Spring service that came back as its first method scores as three-quarters intact.
+  const methods = [];
+  for (let i = 0; i < 12; i++) {
+    methods.push(`  public Account findById${i}(Long id) {`);
+    methods.push('    return repository.findById(id)');
+    methods.push('      .orElseThrow(NotFoundException::new);');
+    methods.push('  }');
+  }
+  const SERVICE = ['package com.example.account.service;', '', '@Service', 'public class AccountService {', ...methods, '}'].join('\n') + '\n';
+  const firstMethodOnly = SERVICE.split('\n').slice(0, 8).join('\n') + '\n';
+  ok(assessLineLoss(SERVICE, firstMethodOnly).kept <= 6,
+    'a repeated line present once in the new body is credited once, not once per copy in the original');
+  ok(/of \d+ existing line\(s\) are not in the new body/.test(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: SERVICE, body: firstMethodOnly, path: 'src/AccountService.java' }).notes.join(' ')),
+    'so a service that lost eleven of its twelve methods REPORTS the true count — the multiplicity fix still matters, because the number it reports has to be honest');
+
+  // The refusal has to name a recovery the model can perform. "Read it again" is satisfied by a SLICE, which
+  // never records anything — the model reads, is refused with the identical message, and reads again. For a file
+  // over the 2MB read ceiling no read can clear it at all, and the only way out is <edit>, which the message
+  // never mentions. A refusal with no reachable exit is worse than no check.
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  const M = '/proj/m.js';
+  const M1 = Array.from({ length: 12 }, (_, i) => `export const K${i} = ${i};`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const M2 = M1 + 'export const FROM_COLLEAGUE = 1;\n';
+  noteServedFile(M, M1);
+  const refusal = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: M2, body: M1.replace('K1 = 1;', 'K1 = 111;'), path: 'src/m.js', absPath: M });
+  ok(!refusal.ok && /whole/i.test(refusal.error || ''),
+    'the refusal says a WHOLE-file read is what clears it — a slice satisfies "read it again" and changes nothing, which is the loop');
+  ok(/edit/i.test(refusal.error || ''),
+    'and it names <edit> as the other way through, because on a file too large to re-read that is the only one');
+
+  // An <edit> re-notes the file from disk, so one unrelated edit makes the record current while the MODEL is
+  // still holding the copy it read before the file moved. The next whole-file write from that stale copy goes
+  // through clean. Pinned as it stands: the record tracks the disk, and this module cannot see what the model
+  // is holding, so closing it means keeping something the edit path does not currently keep.
+  noteServedFile(M, M2.replace('K1 = 1;', 'K1 = 111;'));   // what edit_file records after an unrelated edit
+  const laundered = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: M2.replace('K1 = 1;', 'K1 = 111;'), body: M1.replace('K1 = 1;', 'K1 = 111;'), path: 'src/m.js', absPath: M });
+  ok(laundered.ok, 'an edit re-notes the file, and this module then has nothing left to object to — the record is of the DISK, never of what the model is holding');
+
+  // Truncation is judged on the REPAIRED body, and that ordering is the only reason a mangled-but-complete write
+  // survives at all. Section [1] uses a five-line file, which is under minLost and so cannot fire the truncation
+  // check whichever order the two run in — swap the calls in guardWholeFileWrite today and all of [1]-[6] stays
+  // green while every escaped write of a real-sized file starts getting refused.
+  const FORTY = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => `export const K${i} = "a<b|c>d ${i}";`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const FORTY_ESC = FORTY.replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;');
+  const rOrder = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FORTY, body: FORTY_ESC, path: 'src/ids.ts' });
+  ok(rOrder.ok && rOrder.body === FORTY_ESC, 'a complete forty-line file that arrived fully escaped is written AS SENT');
+  // As authored this asserted the as-arrived body would be REFUSED, which stopped being true when truncation
+  // gained its size condition: entity-escaping makes a body LONGER, so an escaped write can no longer look
+  // truncated. The ordering still matters and is still worth pinning — just on the survival score, which is what
+  // the ordering actually protects, rather than on a refusal that a later fix made unreachable.
+  // ORDERING STOPPED MATTERING when the repair went: nothing rewrites the body, so nothing can run before or
+  // after anything else. What is still worth pinning is that BOTH facts reach the model — every line changed,
+  // and every line would match un-escaped — because either alone reads as a different problem.
+  ok(assessLineLoss(FORTY, FORTY_ESC).ratio === 0 && /40 of 40 existing line\(s\)/.test(rOrder.notes.join(' ')),
+    'the loss is reported in full');
+  ok(/would match the file exactly without them/.test(rOrder.notes.join(' ')),
+    'AND so is the escaping — a body reported only as "lost 40 lines" would send the model rewriting a file that is merely mangled');
+  const escapedAndCut = FORTY_ESC.split('\n').slice(0, 3).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const rBoth = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FORTY, body: escapedAndCut, path: 'src/ids.ts' });
+  // BOTH signals reach the model and neither is suppressed: 40 of 40 lines gone (it is escaped, so none match)
+  // AND the three that arrived are flagged as escaped. The CUT itself is caught upstream by the unterminated
+  // tag; this layer's job is to describe what it sees, which is exactly what it does here.
+  ok(rBoth.ok && /40 of 40 existing line\(s\)/.test(rBoth.notes.join(' ')) && /would match the file exactly without them/.test(rBoth.notes.join(' ')),
+    'and a body that was BOTH escaped and cut off reports both facts — either alone would send the model at the wrong problem');
+
+  // The two constants are the whole difference between "a rewrite the developer has to argue with" and "a file
+  // silently halved". Section [3] samples deep inside both regions, so flipping < to <= or minLost to 11 moves
+  // the line with every assertion still green. Both edges, exactly.
+  const uniq = (n) => Array.from({ length: n }, (_, i) => `export const Q${i} = ${i * 3 + 1};`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const head = (t, n) => t.split('\n').slice(0, n).join('\n') + '\n';
+  ok(assessLineLoss(uniq(40), head(uniq(40), 10)).refuse === false,
+    'keeping exactly a quarter of the lines is allowed — the floor is the point below which we refuse, not at');
+  ok(assessLineLoss(uniq(13), head(uniq(13), 3)).lost === 10 && assessLineLoss(uniq(13), head(uniq(13), 3)).refuse === false,
+    'losing exactly ten lines refuses NOTHING — the constants that used to live here are gone, and with them the edge nobody could set correctly');
+  ok(assessLineLoss(uniq(13), head(uniq(13), 4)).refuse === false,
+    'losing nine is not, however bad the ratio — small files stay out of it, which is what keeps short configs writable');
+
+  // Line endings must not change the verdict, and today they do not: scorable() trims each line so the \r never
+  // reaches the comparison. Worth pinning because this is a Windows checkout — if scoring ever stopped trimming,
+  // the guard would refuse or "repair" essentially every write in the repo. Note the module is of two minds
+  // about it: the digest below is byte-exact, so the same two files are "the same" to scoring and "moved" to the
+  // stale check. Nothing exploits that only because reads serve raw bytes — the BOM case above is the same
+  // read/write asymmetry already firing.
+  const LF = Array.from({ length: 40 }, (_, i) => `export const L${i} = ${i};`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const CRLF = LF.replace(/\n/g, '\r\n');
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: CRLF, body: LF, path: 'src/l.ts' }).notes.length === 0,
+    'a CRLF file rewritten with LF endings is unchanged as far as the guard is concerned — line endings are not content');
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: LF, body: CRLF, path: 'src/l.ts' }).notes.length === 0, 'and the same in reverse, which is what a Windows editor does on save');
+  const oneEdit = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: CRLF, body: LF.replace('L7 = 7;', 'L7 = 77;'), path: 'src/l.ts' });
+  ok(oneEdit.ok && /1 of 40 existing line\(s\)/.test(oneEdit.notes.join(' ')), 'while a real one-line change across a line-ending switch still reports exactly one line');
+  _resetServedFiles();
+  noteServedFile('/proj/l.ts', CRLF);
+  ok(checkStaleBase('/proj/l.ts', LF).stale === true, 'the stale digest, by contrast, is byte-exact — the two halves of this module disagree about whether line endings matter');
+
+  // "The response came back blank" is a transport failure, not an edit, and it is the cheapest input to get
+  // wrong — a refactor that let an empty body skip scoring would delete files silently. The nine-versus-ten
+  // boundary is pinned too, so that wiping a small file stays a deliberate accepted risk rather than an oversight.
+  for (const [body, what] of [['', 'an empty body'], ['   \n\t\n  ', 'a whitespace-only body'], [undefined, 'a missing body'], [null, 'a null body']]) {
+    const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: LF, body, path: 'src/l.ts' });
+    // The reason changed with the mechanism: this used to be caught by the line-survival threshold ("only 0 of
+    // 40 lines"), which is gone. It is now its own categorical rule — not "too little survived" but "nothing was
+    // sent at all" — which is the right shape for it, since there is no percentage to argue about.
+    ok(!r.ok && /the body was empty/.test(r.error || ''), `${what} against a real file is refused, not treated as a request to empty it`);
+  }
+  const nine = Array.from({ length: 9 }, (_, i) => `export const N${i} = ${i};`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  // An empty body is refused at ANY size now. It used to depend on the file having ten lines, which meant a
+  // nine-line file could be silently emptied — a size-dependent rule for something that was never about size.
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: nine, body: '', path: 'src/n.ts' }).ok === false, 'a nine-line file cannot be emptied either — the empty-body rule has no size condition');
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: nine + 'export const N9 = 9;\n', body: '', path: 'src/n.ts' }).ok === false, 'and neither can a ten-line one, for the same single reason');
+
+  // The one input where the repair's premise inverts: a fixture whose CONTENT is entities. Un-escaping destroys
+  // it as surely as leaving it destroys a mangled source file, and the scoring gets it right — worth locking,
+  // because "just decode entities when we see them" is the obvious shortcut and it would break exactly this.
+  const FIXTURE = [
+    '<!-- golden output for the escaper test -->',
+    'const EXPECTED = "&lt;div class=&quot;row&quot;&gt;";',
+    'const AMPERSAND = "&amp;";',
+    'const APOSTROPHE = "&#39;";',
+    'const CLOSING = "&lt;/div&gt;";',
+  ].join('\n') + '\n';
+  const rFix = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FIXTURE, body: FIXTURE.replace('row', 'col'), path: 'test/fixtures/escaped.js' });
+  ok(rFix.ok && !rFix.repaired && rFix.body.includes('&lt;div class=&quot;col&quot;&gt;'),
+    'a fixture whose content IS entities is edited without being "repaired" into the markup it is supposed to describe');
+  const doubled = FIXTURE.replace(/&/g, '&amp;').replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;');
+  const rDbl = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: FIXTURE, body: doubled, path: 'test/fixtures/escaped.js' });
+  // Double-escaping used to be peeled back one layer. Nothing is peeled now — the fixture lands as sent and the
+  // note offers the one-layer form, which is the right call for a file whose CONTENT is entities: only its author
+  // knows whether the outer layer was the transport or the point of the fixture.
+  ok(rDbl.ok && rDbl.body === doubled && /would match the file exactly without them/.test(rDbl.notes.join(' ')),
+    'and when the transport escapes that same fixture a second time it is peeled back to exactly one layer, not zero');
+  ok(!/backslash/.test(rFix.notes.join(' ')),
+    'and the reason it gives is about the file in front of it — blaming backslashes in a file that contains none is a note the model then acts on');
+
+  // The guard runs synchronously before anything reaches disk and costs scorable-lines x body-length, so it is
+  // slowest on exactly the big generated files where a truncated reply is likeliest. Measured 33ms at 256KB,
+  // 2.1s at 2MB — four times per doubling. A bound, so a change to the scoring cannot quietly make it minutes.
+  const lines = [];
+  while (lines.length * 62 < 500000) lines.push(`export function helper${lines.length}(a, b) { return a * ${lines.length + 2} + b; }`);
+  const HALF_MEG = lines.join('\n') + '\n';
+  const t0 = Date.now();
+  assessLineLoss(HALF_MEG, HALF_MEG.replace('helper0(', 'helper0x('));
+  ok(Date.now() - t0 < 3000, 'scoring half a megabyte stays under three seconds — every whole-file write pays this before anything hits disk');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[7] the guard stays linear, because it runs before every write\n');
+{
+  // It was QUADRATIC: line survival did a substring scan of the whole body per line, costing 2154ms on a 2MB
+  // file and 5454ms when the repair path scored twice. Every whole-file write paid that before anything reached
+  // disk, and it scaled the wrong way — slowest on exactly the large generated files where a truncated reply is
+  // likeliest. Counting with a Map made it linear as a side effect of fixing the repeated-line hole.
+  //
+  // The bound is deliberately loose. This is wall-clock on a shared machine, so it is not a benchmark; it is a
+  // tripwire for a return to quadratic, which would blow through it by more than an order of magnitude.
+  const lines = []; let len = 0;
+  for (let i = 0; len < 2 * 1024 * 1024; i++) { const l = `export const R${i} = /a<b|c>d/g; // item ${i}`; lines.push(l); len += l.length + 1; }
+  const src = lines.join('\n');
+
+  const t1 = Date.now();
+  assessLineLoss(src, src.replace('R0 = ', 'R0 = /* edited */ '));
+  const scoreMs = Date.now() - t1;
+  ok(scoreMs < 2000, `scoring a 2MB file takes ${scoreMs}ms — under two seconds, against 2154ms when it was quadratic`);
+
+  const t2 = Date.now();
+  const r = guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: src, body: src.replace(/</g, '&lt;').replace(/>/g, '&gt;'), path: 'big.ts' });
+  const repairMs = Date.now() - t2;
+  ok(repairMs < 3000, `scanning a fully-escaped 2MB body takes ${repairMs}ms — the path that used to cost 5454ms`);
+  ok(r.notes.join(' ').includes('would match the file exactly without them'), 'and it still REPORTS at that size — the scan has to reach the end of a big file, not merely finish quickly');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write('\n[8] a real refactor is not a truncation, however little of the original survives\n');
+{
+  // FROM A LIVE SONNET RUN, 2026-08-14. It was asked to clean up a messy 33-line server.js, did it correctly —
+  // verified by running the finished app — and came within ONE surviving line of being refused. The file's END
+  // STATE was already past the floor, and it only got there by splitting the work into a write plus two edits;
+  // the same correct job done in one whole-file write would have been blocked.
+  //
+  // "Clean up this file" is the ask this branch will see most, and a rewrite legitimately keeps almost nothing.
+  // What separates it from the failure this check exists for is SIZE: a truncated reply is a file cut short,
+  // dramatically smaller than what it replaced. The refactor came back at 74 lines against 33.
+  // The first answer to this was a SIZE test — refuse only if the body also shrank — which is a better guess but
+  // still a guess. It is gone with the rest of the threshold: nothing at this layer distinguishes the two, so it
+  // stops pretending to. Both cases below pass here, and the truncated one is caught upstream on its tag.
+  const MESSY = Array.from({ length: 33 }, (_, i) => `  var legacyThing${i} = doTheOldStyleThing(${i}); // and a comment`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const REWRITTEN = Array.from({ length: 74 }, (_, i) => `const modernThing${i} = doItProperly(${i});`).join('\n') + '\n';
+  const refactor = assessLineLoss(MESSY, REWRITTEN);
+  ok(refactor.ratio === 0 && !refactor.refuse,
+    'a total rewrite keeps NONE of the original and is allowed — the live run that prompted this was one line from being blocked');
+
+  const cut = MESSY.split('\n').slice(0, 3).join('\n') + '\n';
+  ok(!assessLineLoss(MESSY, cut).refuse,
+    'and a truncation is allowed HERE too, because by this measure it is the same thing — pretending otherwise is what cost us the refactor');
+
+  // Which is only tolerable because the real check exists elsewhere and is certain rather than statistical.
+  ok(guardWholeFileWrite({ oldText: MESSY, body: cut, path: 'src/server.js' }).ok === true,
+    'the guard passes both, and truncation is refused by the PARSER seeing a <write> that never closed — see test-tool-parser and the write path in tools.mjs');
+}
+
+process.stdout.write(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed\n`);
+process.exit(fail ? 1 : 0);

=== add test/test-write-liberal.mjs
new 68a90f78cc5196d8ba76ccb5
+/**
+ * write tool — liberal parsing (regression for the E2E finding that Claude/Sonnet emits the
+ * write body as nested <path>/<content> instead of the path="..." attribute → 'path is required'
+ * → flailing). The fix recovers the nested shape; the correct shape stays byte-identical.
+ *
+ * Run: node test/test-write-liberal.mjs
+ */
+import { mkdtemp, rm, readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
+import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
+import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
+import path from 'node:path';
+// executeForModel, aliased: these assert each tool's ERROR TEXT, which is what the model reads. execute() itself
+// now THROWS for a call that cannot be made (inside a <js> snippet that is what a failure is) — see tools.mjs.
+import { executeForModel as execute } from '../src/agent/tools.mjs';
+
+let pass = 0, fail = 0;
+const ok = (c, l) => { if (c) { pass++; console.log('  ok', l); } else { fail++; console.log('  FAIL', l); } };
+const read = (dir, f) => readFile(path.join(dir, f), 'utf8');
+
+async function main() {
+  const dir = await mkdtemp(path.join(tmpdir(), 'cb-wlib-'));
+  try {
+    // (1) the correct format still works exactly as before (parity)
+    await execute('write_file', { path: 'normal.js', content: 'module.exports = 1;\n' }, {}, { cwd: dir });
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(dir, 'normal.js')) && (await read(dir, 'normal.js')) === 'module.exports = 1;\n', 'correct path="..." format writes unchanged');
+
+    // (2) Sonnet's nested <path>/<content> shape is recovered
+    await execute('write_file', { content: '<path>nested.js</path><content>module.exports = 2;</content>' }, {}, { cwd: dir });
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(dir, 'nested.js')) && (await read(dir, 'nested.js')) === 'module.exports = 2;', 'nested <path>/<content> shape recovers path + content');
+
+    // (3) nested <path> with the body after it (no <content> wrapper)
+    await execute('write_file', { content: '<path>bare.js</path>\nactual body here\n' }, {}, { cwd: dir });
+    ok(existsSync(path.join(dir, 'bare.js')) && /actual body here/.test(await read(dir, 'bare.js')), 'nested <path> + trailing body recovers (strips the path tag)');
+
+    // (4) genuinely missing path still errors (no false recovery)
+    const r4 = await execute('write_file', { content: 'just some content, no path anywhere' }, {}, { cwd: dir });
+    ok(/path is required|error/i.test(JSON.stringify(r4)), 'truly missing path still errors (no spurious recovery)');
+  } finally {
+    await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  }
+  console.log(`\n${pass} passed, ${fail} failed`);
+  process.exit(fail === 0 ? 0 : 1);
+}
+main().catch((e) => { console.error('FATAL', e?.stack || e); process.exit(2); });
+
=== end
